# Zircon Hub Responsive Component System

Zircon Hub keeps Slint as the rendering and interaction layer. It does not embed HTML, CSS, WebView, or a browser layout engine. The Hub UI now exposes a Slint-native component layer that borrows the useful parts of Material UI's component taxonomy: tokens first, layout primitives second, then surfaces, inputs, navigation, data display, and overlays.

## Hub Visual Consistency Contract

### `hub-ui-polish-foundations` Milestone Boundary

The workflow milestone `.opencode/workflows/20260528_190023_866_继续完善hub/hub-ui-polish-foundations/decomposition.md` owns only the lower Hub design-system foundation. Its owner files are `tokens.slint`, `theme.slint`, `material_bridge.slint`, `layout.slint`, `surfaces.slint`, `inputs.slint`, `text_input_components.slint`, `input_state_components.slint`, `navigation.slint`, `data_display.slint`, `list_container_components.slint`, `table_view_components.slint`, `tree_view_components.slint`, `operation_timeline_components.slint`, `overlays.slint`, `dropdown_components.slint`, `drawer_window_components.slint`, `button_components.slint`, `icon_button_components.slint`, `shared.slint`, `components.slint`, and the focused static UI contracts named in the document header. This milestone intentionally does not polish shell placement or individual page layouts; those remain downstream work for `hub-ui-polish-shell-responsive`, `hub-ui-polish-project-pages`, and `hub-ui-polish-workspace-pages` after the primitive APIs are stable.

Foundation primitives must expose semantic state instead of page-local visual forks. Tokens define spacing, page padding, panel/toolbar gaps, breakpoints, row/control heights, radii, borders, focus-ring width/color/offset, disabled opacity, Material typography usage, and neutral/info/success/warning/error status colors. Inputs, buttons, navigation rows, and data rows expose shared enabled/disabled, selected or active, focus, status/tone, density, basis/min-width, overflow, and scroll-budget APIs; hover and pressed behavior stays delegated to Material controls or `StateLayerArea` instead of direct `TouchArea` layers. `components.slint` remains a thin import surface, and `shared.slint` is limited to cross-page DTOs plus genuinely shared compatibility wrappers.

Acceptance for this milestone is the focused command set recorded in the document header: `ui_foundation_contract`, `ui_global_rules_contract`, `ui_material_usage_contract`, `ui_typography_contract`, `ui_inputs_contract`, `ui_navigation_contract`, `ui_data_display_contract`, `ui_panel_slot_contract`, and `cargo check -p zircon_hub --locked`. Downstream shell/page polish may consume these token and primitive contracts, but should not rename or bypass them without updating the same owner files and contract tests.

`docs/ui-and-layout/hub.png` is the Projects Dashboard pixel reference. The React/MUI theme tokens in `zircon_hub/web/src/theme/tokens.ts` define the shared reference canvas, radius, density, panel, outline, and state-color values that pages consume through shared Hub components.

The companion `1568x1003` design PNG set under `docs/ui-and-layout/` is now AI-directed, HTML/CSS-finalized. `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-ai-reference-manifest.json` records `docs/ui-and-layout/hub.png` as the source reference, the prompt family, selected page draft role, AI structure-layout direction drafts, and final export filenames. `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-ai-reference-manifest.schema.json` is the manifest schema contract, and `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-web-reference/validate-visuals.mjs` runs schema subset validation plus negative schema self-tests for extra fields, missing reference output, short reference inventory, malformed AI draft paths, and canvas drift. Those AI drafts are for reviewing the overall interaction structure first: navigation, page regions, toolbars, card/table grids, side panels, dropdowns, modals, and state overlays. local functional-content callouts are secondary and only clarify page intent. `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-web-reference/export-pages.mjs` captures the static web reference with Playwright/Edge, so the final reference text, icons, spacing, menus, overlays, and state panels are authored in HTML/CSS. The exporter starts on port `5198` by default, falls back to a free local port when the default is occupied, and keeps explicit `ZIRCON_HUB_WEB_REFERENCE_PORT` strict. `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-web-reference/validate-interactions.mjs` then locks browser-openable page selection: it rejects unknown `data-route` and `href="?page=..."` targets, verifies exported PNG filename replay, verifies every `EXPORTS.md` replay path, and checks representative header, project, toolbar, and Quick Actions click routes. AI drafts are design direction only and are not acceptance evidence; real Hub captures remain runtime evidence and must still be collected with the Hub screenshot scripts before claiming runtime visual acceptance.

The supplemental design-mode sheets `hub-design-structure-layout.png`, `hub-design-structure-supplement.png`, and `hub-design-functional-details.png` stay outside the final `hub-*.png` acceptance inventory. They exist for overall structure layout review and local functional-content detail discussion only; the accepted page/state references remain the manifest outputs plus `hub.png`. Their reproducible source is `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-design-board/index.html`, exported with `node docs/ui-and-layout/hub-design-board/export-design-board.mjs` and checked with `node docs/ui-and-layout/hub-design-board/validate-design-board.mjs`, including the structure-review checklist and coverage matrix.

`ui_visual_standard_contract` locks the exported visual spec, required design PNG list, reference canvas size, AI manifest coverage, web exporter coverage, documentation links, and the `docs/ui-and-layout/index.md` artifact matrix that maps each design PNG to its page/state and runtime screenshot path.

The same contract locks root page composition. `project_dashboard.slint`, Projects secondary pages, `editor.slint`, `builds.slint`, `assets.slint`, `plugins.slint`, `cloud.slint`, `team.slint`, `learn.slint`, and `settings.slint` must each expose one root page component, keep local code to layout/data/callback forwarding, and compose shared wrappers such as `DashboardToolbar`, `ProjectBrowserResultsPanel`, `EditorActionsPanel`, `BuildTaskHistoryPanel`, `CatalogPage`, `CloudServicesPanel`, `TeamMembersPanel`, `SettingsToolchainPanel`, and `OperationTimelinePanel`. New visual helpers belong in page component modules or shared token/component layers, not in root pages.

The final Hub visual audit also keeps raw page colors centralized: `theme.slint` and `tokens.slint` are the only UI files allowed to define direct color literals, and page/shell components route status colors through `HubVisualSpec.success-stroke`, `accent-stroke`, `warning-stroke`, `error-stroke`, and outline tokens instead of private `rgb()` branches.

The 2026-05-29 takeover capture set at `target/hub-visual-check-final/` validates the same `1568x1003` runtime canvas against the new visual standard. Root captures cover `hub-projects-dashboard.png`, `hub-projects-new-project.png`, `hub-projects-browser.png`, `hub-projects-detail.png`, `hub-projects-detail-delete-confirm.png`, `hub-projects-browser-filter-menu.png`, and `hub-projects-browser-sort-menu.png`; `target/hub-visual-check-final/main-pages/` covers `hub-editor.png`, `hub-builds.png`, `hub-assets.png`, `hub-plugins.png`, `hub-cloud.png`, `hub-team.png`, `hub-learn.png`, and `hub-settings.png`; `target/hub-visual-check-final/popups/` covers `hub-source-engine-popup.png` and `hub-user-menu.png`; `target/hub-visual-check-final/states/` covers `hub-state-empty.png`, `hub-state-loading.png`, and `hub-state-error.png`. The final screenshot check verified every PNG in this set is `1568x1003` and non-empty; `hub-learn.stderr.log` contains only repeated ICU4X segmentation-model warnings and did not block image output.

The 2026-05-29 responsive acceptance capture set at `target/hub-visual-check-responsive-0529/` extends that evidence to the planned `1600x1024`, `1280x900`, and `1024x720` breakpoints. Each breakpoint folder contains Projects Dashboard/New/Browser/Detail, browser filter/sort menus, and delete-confirm captures at the root, plus main navigation and top-header popup screenshots under `main-pages/`. The project-page script now seeds `recent_projects.path` and `project_metadata` keys with the real isolated project directories under `project-pages-config`, so guarded delete captures validate the actual confirmation state instead of a missing-path error. The final responsive check verified all required PNGs match their breakpoint dimensions, are non-empty, and have a visible Detail-to-delete-confirm delta; it also verified every seeded project path exists. `hub-learn.stderr.log` again contains only the known repeated ICU4X segmentation-model warning.

Manual visual acceptance remains stricter than file existence. A Hub runtime capture is accepted only when it preserves component density against the matching design PNG, has no overlapping UI, no clipped text or controls, consistent button and badge states, stable panel hierarchy, and the shared empty/loading/error styling. `ui_visual_standard_contract` keeps this checklist present in the visual docs so future capture updates cannot reduce acceptance to dimensions and non-empty PNG checks.

The global-state design PNGs (`hub-state-empty.png`, `hub-state-loading.png`, and `hub-state-error.png`) are HTML/CSS-finalized baselines and now have matching `1568x1003` runtime captures in `target/hub-visual-check-final/states/`. The empty capture uses an isolated Hub profile with no recent projects and validates the shared `EmptyStateBlock`/`EmptyStatePanel` treatment. The loading and error captures use an isolated selected project on the Builds page: loading captures `TaskStatus::running_operation` while a background source build is still running, and error captures the recoverable Source Engine missing-directory path through `TaskStatus::error`/`StatusBanner`. They remain in the design matrix so page implementations can compare state visuals without inventing page-local empty, loading, or error styling.

The Hub now imports the local Slint Material UI template directly. `zircon_hub/build.rs` registers `dev/material-rust-template/material-1.0/material.slint` as the Slint library path `@material`, and `material_bridge.slint` re-exports the template's layout, card, button, input, navigation, list, progress, tab, tooltip, and style primitives for Hub pages that need the real template components. `theme.slint` installs a Zircon teal/dark `MaterialPalette.schemes` override at UI initialization time, so Material-owned controls keep the template behavior without inheriting the template's default blue palette. This is a direct template dependency, not a hand-written Material clone and not an HTML/CSS engine.

Hub surfaces keep the Zircon dark tool UI contract, but they are now backed by real Material template cards where the template provides the matching primitive. `HubPanel` selects `ElevatedCard` for elevated variants and `OutlinedCard` for standard panel variants, then hosts Hub-owned border, tint, and children content so existing page APIs continue to work while the base surface comes from the imported template. Panel wrappers do not add direct pointer layers above children; nested list rows, buttons, inputs, and detail affordances own their interaction through Material controls, `ListTile`, or `StateLayerArea`.

Shared Hub button APIs also delegate to the imported Material template instead of reimplementing pointer behavior locally. `PillButton` wraps `FilledButton` for primary actions and `TonalButton` for secondary actions, while Hub `IconButton` wraps `FilledIconButton` for active icon actions and `OutlineIconButton` for inactive icon actions. `WindowButton` wraps the template `IconButton` with the compact inline icon metric and the template error color path for the close action, so title-bar minimize/maximize/close controls use the same centered icon layout as other Material icon controls instead of a custom `TouchArea`. `PillButton` owns a stable atom-level preferred width and clip boundary so Material text buttons cannot overpaint adjacent responsive header actions at compact window sizes; Hub `IconButton` clips the Material icon button to the requested atom size for dense rows. All three shared button wrappers now expose explicit `fallback-icon-image` inputs, token-derived focus radii, Hub focus overlays, shared disabled opacity, and enabled-gated callbacks. The Hub-facing properties remain stable (`text`, `icon-image`, `fallback-icon-image`, `has-icon-image`, `primary`, `active`, `enabled`, `focused`, and `clicked`), so existing pages keep semantic button usage while hover, disabled, ripple, accessibility, and icon button behavior come from the direct Material template import.

Hub text input fields now follow the same compatibility-wrapper pattern. `HubTextField` lives in `text_input_components.slint`, exposes Hub-facing `label`, `placeholder`, `supporting-text`, `text`, `enabled`, derived `focused`, and edit/accept callbacks, and renders the imported Material template `TextField` internally. The same focused text-input owner also keeps `SearchBox` and `HubPathFieldRow`, so compact search, Material text fields, and path rows can evolve without growing the select/dropdown owner. The wrapper owns the Hub disabled opacity and tokenized focus-ring overlay (`HubVisualSpec.focus-ring-width` / `focus-ring-color`) while the Material field owns text editing, label, placeholder, supporting text, and icon layout. Settings uses this wrapper for build jobs and routes toolchain/default-path rows through exported Settings-specific wrappers in `settings_page_components.slint`; Editor uses it for active engine name, source checkout, and staged output fields; Projects New Project routes project name and the editable project creation location through `ProjectCreateSettingsPanel`, which owns the exported `ProjectCreateField` rows from `project_page_components.slint`, so both fields share the same `HubTextField` binding shape while the location row alone enables the Browse button and forwards `browse-folder("new-project-location")`.

Projects New Project and Project Detail also route registered Source Engine choices through exported `ProjectEngineChoiceList`; the wrapper owns the shared `PanelListViewport`, `EngineChoiceRow`, empty-state block, scroll binding, and selected-engine callback forwarding, while each page or enclosing panel supplies its own list height, row metrics, empty-state copy, compact-label rule, and target selection callback. New Project template choices route through exported `ProjectTemplateRailPanel`; the wrapper inherits `PanelSlot`, owns the template rail `PanelHeader`, Material `PanelListViewport`, repeated `TemplateChoiceRow` delegates, scroll binding, and selected-template callback forwarding, while `ProjectNewPage` keeps only the rail's responsive basis/grow/min-width/height, list height, row metrics, localized title/availability copy, projected template data, and compact-label rule. `TemplateChoiceRow` now renders the selected template through the shared `HubCheckBox` primitive plus `StateLayerArea`, `MaterialText`, and `StatusBadge`, so template selection exercises the same Material checkbox atom as future binary rows without reintroducing page-local pointer handling.

Project Detail's project information list routes through exported `ProjectDetailInfoSection` from `project_detail_components.slint`, which owns the shared `PanelHeader` plus the five `ProjectSettingSummaryRow` bindings for status, root path, Source Engine, engine version, and last modified while the page supplies only section height, row metrics, compact subtitle state, projected detail data, and localized copy. Project Detail command actions now consume the shared `HubActionCommandButton` primitive directly inside `ProjectDetailActionStack` and `ProjectDetailDeleteActionStack`, so open, remove from Hub, delete, confirm delete, and cancel delete share the same Material-backed command-button geometry, icon binding, disabled opacity, focus ring, and state-layer path as other Hub action rows. `ProjectDetailActionsSection` now inherits `HubContentPanelSlot`, so the Project Actions header and body spacing come from the shared content-panel shell. The component still owns page-specific callback routing, the delete-confirmation branch, `ProjectDetailPinToggleRow`, and `ProjectDetailEngineSection`, but it no longer keeps a pass-through `ProjectDetailActionButton` wrapper, a local `PillButton` action shell, or a local `PanelHeader` declaration.

That New Project location is stored as `HubRuntime::new_project_location`, projected through `HubSnapshot`, and bound to `HubWindow.new-project-location`; it intentionally does not share the Settings `project-location` property that persists `HubSettings.default_project_dir`, so selecting a template, selecting an engine, or refreshing a snapshot cannot overwrite the address currently being edited on the New Project page. The New Project create action and Create Summary badge now require a non-empty project name, a non-empty project location, an enabled template, and a selected Source Engine before showing the ready state; the runtime still performs the authoritative trimmed-name/path validation before launching the Editor create command. Hub editable form pages should not import `std-widgets` `LineEdit` directly, while `HubTokens.input-field` owns the Material field atom height and `HubTokens.input-width` gives Material text fields a stable preferred width before row stretch is available. Toolbar search remains exposed as the Hub-facing `SearchBox` API, but it intentionally uses the reference Hub outlined shell plus a compact `TextInput` and search SVG so dashboard/browser toolbars match the `hub.png` density instead of returning to the earlier Material capsule/search-field branch.

Projects New Project's settings panel now routes through exported `ProjectCreateSettingsPanel` from `project_page_components.slint`. The typed panel inherits `HubContentPanelSlot`, so its settings header and content stack use the same shared Material CardHeader/CardContent-style shell as other Hub content panels, while the component still owns the project-name and location `ProjectCreateField` rows, the Source Engine label plus `ProjectEngineChoiceList`, the `ProjectCreateActionRow`, and the desktop create summary. The compact stacked summary routes through `ProjectCreateCompactSummaryPanel`, which owns the separate compact panel shell and delegates visible rows to `ProjectCreateSummary`; `ProjectCreateSummary` inherits `HubSection`, so the summary header no longer repeats a local `PanelHeader` plus `VerticalLayout` stack. The page supplies only responsive basis/grow/min-width/height mirrors, form-ready state, localized `UiTextData`, row/list/summary metrics, scroll bindings, project data, and callback forwarding. The same component module owns `ProjectTemplateRailPanel`, so the template rail is no longer an inline `PanelSlot`/`PanelHeader`/`PanelListViewport` block inside `project_new_page.slint`, and `ProjectNewPage` no longer directly imports or calls `PanelSlot`. `ui_project_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_inputs_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs`, `ui_project_navigation_contract.rs`, and `ui_typography_contract.rs` lock these rows and panel boundaries so the form panel does not return to an inline `HorizontalLayout`/`PillButton` action cluster, the Source Engine list does not return to page-local scroll wiring, the compact summary does not return to a direct page `PanelSlot`, and the template rail does not return to a page-local list viewport. The 2026-05-28 `hub-docs-contracts` refresh also locks the Rust-side create workflow in `project_workflow_contract.rs`: the selected `new_project_engine_id` must be validated, written as the active Source Engine, stored in Hub project metadata with the selected template, and only then passed to `remember_project` so the next Slint snapshot sees selected-project, Source Engine, Builds, Cloud/package, Assets, Plugins, Learn, and Team scope in one refresh.

Project Detail's header metadata strip now routes through exported `ProjectDetailStatusStrip` from `project_detail_components.slint`. The wrapper owns the version badge, pinned/not-pinned badge, modified-time `MaterialText`, and status-row spacing, while `ProjectDetailPage` only supplies row metrics, badge widths, projected detail data, and localized copy. `ui_project_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, and `ui_typography_contract.rs` lock this status-strip shape so the version/pin/modified metadata does not return to loose page-body bindings.

Project Detail's Change Source Engine block now routes through exported `ProjectDetailEngineSection` from `project_detail_components.slint`. The wrapper owns the local `PanelHeader`, current bound-engine subtitle, shared `ProjectEngineChoiceList`, scroll binding, row metrics, empty-state copy, and selected-engine callback forwarding, while the actions panel only decides whether the section is visible outside pending delete. `ui_project_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, and `ui_typography_contract.rs` lock this section so the actions panel does not re-expand the engine-change list as a loose `VerticalLayout`.

The Projects secondary-page wrapper components now live outside the page roots instead of at the top of `project_pages.slint`. `project_page_components.slint` exports shared secondary-page components (`PageHeader`, `ProjectCreateSettingsPanel`, `ProjectCreateCompactSummaryPanel`, `ProjectCreateField`, `ProjectCreateActionRow`, `ProjectEngineChoiceList`, `ProjectTemplateRailPanel`, engine/template rows, and summary rows), `project_detail_components.slint` owns Detail-specific action/status/info/engine-section wrappers, and `project_browser_components.slint` owns Browser-specific `ProjectFilterSelect`, `ProjectSortSelect`, and `ProjectBrowserRow` while remaining re-exported through `project_page_components.slint` for the existing browser import surface. `PageHeader` keeps its back-size and callback contract, but the chevron affordance now renders through the shared `HubBackButton` primitive so Projects secondary pages use the same tokenized icon-button radius, border, foreground, focus ring, disabled opacity, and `StateLayerArea` path as other Hub chrome controls without re-declaring the back icon locally. `project_new_page.slint` owns the New Project page shell, typed settings/compact-summary/template panel call sites, metric calculations, scroll bindings, data forwarding, and callback forwarding; `ProjectCreateSettingsPanel` owns the settings panel's `PanelSlot` body and create-form internals, `ProjectCreateCompactSummaryPanel` owns the compact summary `PanelSlot` body, and `ProjectTemplateRailPanel` owns the template rail's `PanelSlot` body, `PanelHeader`, Material `PanelListViewport`, and row repetition. `project_browser_page.slint` owns the Browser page shell, toolbar, explicit list-panel call site, and inner `browser-scroll-y` forwarding; `project_detail_page.slint` owns the Detail page shell, detail/action `PanelSlot` columns, cover section, guarded delete confirmation, and Detail wrapper call sites. `project_pages.slint` is now only the stable secondary-page aggregate that re-exports `ProjectNewPage`, `ProjectBrowserPage`, and `ProjectDetailPage` so `projects.slint` keeps one import source. The project layout, inputs, Material usage, navigation, page-surface, panel-slot, and typography contracts check both sides of that boundary: shared component definitions stay in `project_page_components.slint`, Browser definitions stay in `project_browser_components.slint`, Detail definitions stay in `project_detail_components.slint`, New page call sites stay in `project_new_page.slint`, Browser page call sites stay in `project_browser_page.slint`, Detail call sites stay in `project_detail_page.slint`, and `project_pages.slint` stays implementation-free.

Hub segmented controls also use the imported template now. The Hub-facing `SegmentButton` wrapper keeps the existing `text`, `active`, and `clicked` API for toolbar and compact Hub tool surfaces, but its internal state is a one-item Material `SegmentedButton` rather than a hand-painted rectangle plus `TouchArea`. The wrapper uses the Material component's 40px minimum control height through `HubTokens.control-md`, so future segmented rows do not clip the template's state layer or selected check affordance. Settings build-profile and language choices now use the `HubComboBox` menu wrapper instead, keeping those configuration choices on the same Material `DropDownMenu` path as other menu-backed selectors.

Toolbar dropdowns now follow the same rule. `HubSelectTrigger` owns the shared MUI-style select anchor: the transparent imported Material `OutlineButton` hit target, Hub focus/disabled state, tokenized chevron, optional leading icon, state layer, and compact/dense label treatment. `ToolbarSelect` keeps the Hub-facing label, icon, legacy option id, and callback contract, but delegates trigger painting to `HubSelectTrigger` and sends popup geometry/items through `HubSelectMenu`. Projects dashboard and browser pages pass both Material `menu-items` and the existing id-bearing `options`, so the visual/menu layer comes from the local template while filter and sort actions still dispatch the stable Hub ids.

The generic Hub `DropDownButton` wrapper is also backed by Material buttons. It now renders an imported `OutlineButton` for inactive dropdown triggers and `TonalButton` for active triggers, using the existing Hub `text`, `icon-image`, `has-icon-image`, `active`, and `clicked` properties. The top Source Engine selector is not a simple `MenuItem` projection, so it keeps a typed engine-list popup, but its anchor now also consumes `HubSelectTrigger`; popup paper, header, list viewport, and rows come from `HubAnchoredPopupPanel`, `HubPopupSectionHeader`, `HeaderEngineOptionsList`, and `HubMenuRow` instead of local border/background/text/chevron assembly.

Collapsed Hub navigation now delegates to the imported Material template `NavigationRail`. Rust still projects the stable Hub `NavItemData` model for page ids, titles, and active state, and `view_model.rs` derives a parallel `NavigationItem` model from the same data for the Material rail. `NavRail` uses that Material model only in collapsed mode and dispatches clicks back through the original Hub page ids by index, so page routing stays unchanged. Expanded navigation remains the Hub-owned left-aligned `NavButton` row list because the template `NavigationRail` is an 80px icon rail and the template `NavigationDrawer` has a wider drawer contract than the Hub's 200px sidebar; each expanded `NavButton` keeps the Hub row shell, tokenized active/focus border, disabled opacity, Material `StateLayerArea` interaction body, icon slot, and MaterialText label. `HubSidebarCollapseButton` in `button_components.slint` now owns the Drawer collapse affordance's Material `StateLayerArea`, chevron/collapse icon mapping, collapsed-label projection, Material label text, and click callback, while the private `HubSidebarCollapseControl` in `shell_sidebar_components.slint` only forwards sidebar state, row height, radius, localized label, foreground, and click dispatch. This leaves the shell's direct `TouchArea` usage for the frameless title-bar drag region only. Both manual collapse and compact auto-collapse feed the same `nav-effective-collapsed` state for rail width, padding, and `NavRail` mode, so compact windows do not keep expanded-sidebar padding around the 80px Material rail. `HubTokens.nav-width-collapsed-*` therefore uses the Material `size_80` rail width instead of the old 64/72px custom icon strip.

`components.slint` is intentionally only a public entrypoint. Existing pages can continue importing from it, but implementation belongs in the category files below:

- `tokens.slint` owns spacing, breakpoints, page padding, panel gaps, toolbar gaps, control heights, icon sizes, list/table row heights, workspace panel row heights, borders, panel minimums, and shared dark Hub colors. The current shared scale is fixed at spacing `0/4/8/12/16/24/28`, panel gap `14px`, toolbar gap `10px`, icon sizes `16/18/20/28/34px`, control heights `34/38/42px`, table rows `28px`, and list rows `56/72/86px`; those values are expressed through `MaterialStyleMetrics` and `HubTokens` rather than raw page literals.
- `material_bridge.slint` owns no Hub styling. It is the only Hub UI file allowed to import `@material` directly, then re-exports the local Material template components that Hub wrappers are allowed to consume through `components.slint` or the bridge itself. Conflicting public names such as Hub `Badge` stay under Hub ownership; badge labels can still use bridged Material typography primitives such as `MaterialText`.
- `layout.slint` owns CSS-like composition primitives: `Stack`, `Row`, `Column`, `Flow`, `FlowScrollSurface`, `Page`, `PanelGrid`, `ResponsiveSlot`, `HubWorkspaceSplitState`, `ResponsiveState`, `Fill`, `Divider`, `PageScrollSurface`, `ResponsivePanelFlow`, and `WorkspacePanelSection`. `Page` uses the Material template `ScrollView`, exposes padded `content-width`, usable `viewport-height`, and padded `content-height`, and delegates `Divider` to Material `HorizontalDivider`/`VerticalDivider`; its default padding is compact-aware through `HubTokens.page-padding-compact` so small routed pages do not need to override page chrome just to reduce edge gutters. `FlowScrollSurface` is the Material-backed viewport for flow-style collections such as the Dashboard project-card rail: callers provide `scroll-y`, `content-width`, `content-height`, and child flow content while the layout layer owns the Material `ScrollView` policies and transparent content surface. `ResponsiveState` centralizes viewport breakpoint booleans (`compact`, `medium`, `wide`, and `short`) so app chrome and page shells consume semantic state instead of scattering `root.width < ...` checks. `HubWorkspaceSplitState` centralizes main/side workspace split state for Editor, Builds, and Settings: content-width breakpoint, main/side basis, compact labels, grow weights, and compact min-width outputs stay in the shared layout layer while page roots keep only row heights, ordering rules, data forwarding, and callbacks. `Fill` is the shell/page-slot fill primitive and owns the internal child `VerticalLayout`, including the selected-page slot in `app.slint`, so page instances stretch into the allocated area instead of repeating `width: parent.width` and `height: parent.height`. `PanelGrid` and `WorkspacePanelSection` now expose token-backed padding slots for rare container inset needs while remaining geometry-only; they must not absorb panel chrome, data rows, buttons, typography, or page-specific business presentation. Content width is still constrained by the Hub shell so the page body only sees the viewport left after the navigation rail.
- `theme.slint` owns the Hub-level Material palette override. It is installed by `HubWindow` through a zero-size `ZirconMaterialTheme` component so every imported Material control uses the same Zircon teal/dark scheme.
- `button_components.slint` owns the Material-backed non-icon button family. `PillButton`, `WindowButton`, and the shared command/action/sidebar/user-menu/panel wrappers preserve Hub-facing semantic APIs while their atom behavior routes through imported Material buttons or Material state layers.
- `icon_button_components.slint` owns the square icon-button family. Hub `IconButton`, `HubIconButton`, topbar/back/flow-next/row-action buttons, view toggles, floating overlay icon buttons, and the more-menu button keep their image, state-layer, focus, color, and geometry contracts in this focused owner. `button_state_sample_components.slint` owns the Dashboard reference/demo button-state samples separately, importing only `HubIconButton`, tokens, and Material text/color primitives from the icon-button foundation it demonstrates.
- `shared.slint` owns shared DTO structs plus compact shared visual helpers such as `BrandMark`, `CenteredIcon`, header `StatusPill`, `Panel`, `FieldLabel`, and `MutedText`. Those wrappers preserve common Hub data/text/status APIs and route their internals through Material `ActionChip` and `MaterialText` where the local template owns the chip behavior and typography metrics.
- `surfaces.slint` owns panel/card/header/badge/banner surfaces. `HubPanel` is the compatibility wrapper around Material `ElevatedCard` and `OutlinedCard`; Hub-specific variants only add the Zircon border/tint layer above that template surface. `PanelSlot` inherits `ResponsiveSlot` and centralizes the standard workspace-panel shell: Material-backed `HubPanel`, padded stretch body, and child slot forwarding. Page use sites keep the semantic basis/grow/shrink/order inputs next to the matching Slint `flex-basis`/`flex-grow`/`flex-shrink`/`flex-order` properties because Slint 1.16.1 only accepts those flex properties on direct `FlexboxLayout` children. `OverviewPanel` centralizes the top summary-card shell used by Cloud and Team, reusing `HubPanel` plus `PanelHeader` instead of repeating page-local padding/layout boilerplate. `EmptyStateBlock` owns the Material `MaterialText`/`MutedText` body for empty states, and `EmptyStatePanel` wraps that block in `HubPanel` for dashboard and typed-list surfaces, so page files pass title/detail/extra-detail and padding instead of repeating `HubPanel`, `VerticalLayout`, `MaterialText`, and `MutedText` blocks. `PanelHeader` keeps the Hub right-action API, but the action now delegates to the shared `HubPanelHeaderActionButton` wrapper while that button owns the imported Material `OutlineButton`, chevron icon, disabled opacity, and click gating in the button family. `PanelHeader` title typography still goes through Material `MaterialText`, and `StatusBanner` titles use the same `MaterialText` path. `Badge` and `StatusBadge` keep the Hub tone shell and public name while delegating label typography and alignment to Material `MaterialText`.
- `inputs.slint` owns select-trigger, toolbar-select, dropdown-button, and segmented controls, plus `SelectOptionData` for id-bearing toolbar select adapters. It keeps the shared `HubSelectTrigger` anchor used by toolbar selects and the top Source Engine selector, the shared `HubSelectMenu`-backed `ToolbarSelect` wrapper, the Material `OutlineButton`/`TonalButton`-backed Hub `DropDownButton` wrapper, and the Material `SegmentedButton`-backed Hub `SegmentButton` wrapper.
- `text_input_components.slint` owns text-entry primitives. It keeps the Hub-outlined `SearchBox` wrapper with compact `TextInput` plus private `SearchBoxPlaceholderText`, the Material `TextField`-backed `HubTextField` wrapper, and the `HubPathFieldRow` composition that pairs a text field with the shared command-button action.
- `input_state_components.slint` owns Material-backed checkbox, checkbox-row, switch, toggle-row, and combo-box wrappers. It imports the Material checkbox/switch primitives through `material_bridge.slint` and imports only `HubSelectDropDownSurface` from `overlays.slint` for combo menu behavior, so binary state and combo controls can evolve without growing the text/select input owner.

The `hub-ui-polish-foundations-inputs-navigation` child tightened that primitive-state boundary. `PillButton`, Hub `IconButton`, `WindowButton`, `DropDownButton`, `SegmentButton`, `SearchBox`, `HubTextField`, and `ToolbarSelect` all expose semantic enabled/focused/active state at the Hub wrapper layer while hover, pressed, ripple, text, and icon behavior stays inside the imported Material controls or `StateLayerArea`. Focus rings are intentionally overlaid by the Hub wrapper because the local Material template does not expose one uniform focus-ring API across filled, tonal, outlined, icon, segmented, text-field, and title-bar button variants; those overlays use `HubVisualSpec.focus-ring-width` and `HubVisualSpec.focus-ring-color` rather than page-local colors. The `hub-ui-polish-foundations-inputs-navigation-button-states` child now splits that button state boundary between `button_components.slint` and `icon_button_components.slint`: `PillButton`, Hub `IconButton`, and `WindowButton` all gate `clicked` callbacks when disabled, use `HubVisualSpec.disabled-opacity`, derive focus radius from Material/token metrics, and expose a real fallback image property so missing caller icons produce a visible alert or close glyph instead of a blank control. Hub `IconButton` delegates active/inactive icon controls to Material `FilledIconButton` and `OutlineIconButton` in the icon-button owner, and `WindowButton` keeps the title-bar controls on Material `IconButton` in the non-icon button owner while honoring the same enabled/focus state contract. `ui_inputs_contract.rs` and `ui_foundation_contract.rs` are the focused acceptance gates for this shared-button primitive slice. `NavRail` keeps expanded navigation on `NavButton` rows owned by `navigation.slint` and collapsed navigation on Material `NavigationRail`; the collapsed rail receives an adapter model derived from the same Rust `NavItemData` projection, and the wrapper gates disabled collapsed interactions by presenting an empty Material item model while expanded rows forward `enabled` into each `NavButton`.

The `hub-ui-polish-foundations-inputs-navigation-input-states` child keeps the input half of that boundary split between `text_input_components.slint`, `inputs.slint`, and `input_state_components.slint`. `SearchBox` now derives its shell background, border, and placeholder tone from private state properties instead of page-level colors: disabled fields use the shared Material disabled alpha, focused fields raise to `surface_container_high` with the Hub focus ring, non-empty fields keep the stronger outline, and empty placeholders remain visible even while the inner `TextInput` is focused. `HubTextField` keeps Material `TextField` as the editing atom but centralizes its Hub focus-border and disabled-opacity values as wrapper state inside the text-input owner. `ToolbarSelect` now has explicit `menu-ready`, `select-background`, `select-border`, and `select-foreground` state so enabled, disabled, populated, empty, focused, and compact toolbar menu triggers use one token-driven visual contract; the transparent Material `OutlineButton` remains the trigger while the visible Hub shell owns the independent chevron, foreground, and `StateLayerArea` menu affordance. `DropDownButton` and `SegmentButton` now expose a shared `button-height` density input and derive focus-ring radius from that height; `SegmentButton` continues to gate click callbacks at the wrapper layer because the current imported template segmented control does not expose an `enabled` property, and its disabled `index_changed` branch immediately restores `selected-index` from the wrapper `active` model so disabled pointer interaction cannot leave a toggled Material visual state behind. `input_state_components.slint` keeps `HubCheckBox`, `HubCheckBoxRow`, `HubSwitch`, `HubToggleRow`, and `HubComboBox` on the same shared enabled/focused/disabled-opacity contract while moving their Material checkbox/switch/combo imports out of `inputs.slint`. `ui_inputs_contract.rs` and `ui_input_primitives_contract.rs` lock these private state helpers, tokenized density hooks, disabled SegmentButton reset behavior, and Material-backed control boundaries so downstream button and navigation milestones can depend on a stable input primitive API.
- `data_display.slint` owns shared data rows, row surfaces, and metric atoms; `list_container_components.slint` owns generic `PanelListViewport`, menu-list viewport, list view, and list panel shells; `table_view_components.slint` owns the table shell layer, and `tree_view_components.slint` owns the tree DTO/row/view layer. `InfoRow`, `ActionRow`, and `BuildHistoryRow` use the shared `HubRowSurface`/`HubInteractiveRowSurface` plus row-slot family for selected/disabled/focus state, Material state layers, icon/avatar lanes, title/detail text, and trailing badge/action affordances. `InfoRow` exposes idle/selected border widths, idle/selected backgrounds, and enabled avatar colors, so popup selector rows can reuse the shared information-row primitive while keeping transparent inactive rows and accent selected rows. `ActionRow` exposes `show-trailing`, disabled-shell opacity, enabled/disabled avatar color overrides, title/detail text overrides, and leading icon size overrides, so compact dashboard/action-list variants can reuse the same row primitive without hand-painting local `StateLayerArea`, `Image`, or `MaterialText` bodies. `BuildHistoryRow` is shared by Editor and Builds so recent source-build records use one data-display implementation instead of a page-local Editor row. `OperationTimelineRowData` stays defined in `shared.slint`, while `operation_timeline_components.slint` owns `OperationTimelineRow` and the typed `OperationTimelinePanel`; the panel now inherits `HubListPanelSlot`, so the title, count badge, shared `PanelListViewport`, scroll binding, row metrics, and empty-state height use the same list-panel shell as other Hub action/status lists while the module keeps only timeline-specific row projection and empty copy. The row projects action record detail text by preferring recovery guidance, then `log_excerpt`, then command line, then plain detail, so persisted build/process errors surface the most actionable text first. `components.slint` re-exports the timeline module so Builds and Settings render the same persisted action-history panel without importing each other. `MetricCard` is the shared Material-text metric/summary card body used by Cloud readiness metrics and Team identity/repository summaries, replacing page-local `CloudMetric`, `TeamSummaryItem`, and `TeamSummaryCard` implementations. `TableColumnHeader`, `ProjectTableRow`, `HubTableBody`, `DataTable`, and `HubTableView` now live together in `table_view_components.slint`, while `TreeItemData`, `HubTreeRow`, and `HubTreeView` now live together in `tree_view_components.slint`; table/tree modules import the row primitives from `data_display.slint` and the shared list viewport from `list_container_components.slint`. `CatalogListPanel`, `PanelListViewport`, and `HubTableBody` use the Material `ScrollView` surface instead of `std-widgets.slint`, so shared table/list scrolling follows the same template path as page-level `PageScrollSurface`. `CatalogPage` now inherits `PageScrollSurface` directly and consumes its `content-height` for the list panel budget, while `CatalogListPanel` derives minimum panel and empty-state heights from the semantic `row-height` parameter. Assets, Plugins, and Learn now inherit `CatalogPage` as their page root instead of wrapping it in a local `Rectangle`, so those pages only set catalog title/badge/empty-state/row semantics and row call sites; the shared page owns geometry, page scroll, and internal list-scroll state. `catalog_page_components.slint` owns the exported catalog row wrappers `AssetRow`, `PluginRow`, and `LearnRow`, keeping per-row `InfoRow` metadata, icons, trailing badges, compact label collapse, and Learn's arrow/open-resource affordance out of the page roots. This keeps catalog pages away from page-local window-height minus padding formulas and row-construction boilerplate while letting taller plugin/learn rows reserve matching space. `MetricCard`, `TableColumnHeader`, `ProjectTableRow`, and the shared `EmptyStateBlock` used by `DataTable` and `CatalogListPanel` now use Material `MaterialText`, keeping proportional metric/column/catalog layout in Hub code while delegating typography metrics to the template. Editor source-engine rows follow the same Material `ListTile` rule while keeping their Hub-specific trailing status and remove controls.
- App chrome implementation lives in focused shell component modules, while `components.slint` remains the public import surface for `HubTopHeader`, `HubNavSidebar`, `HubPageHeader`, and `HubStatusBar`. The former `shell.slint` compatibility note has been deleted so no migration-only shell file remains between callers and the focused owners. Source-engine popup heading/count text, top-header brand and user text, nav current-project forwarding, page-title text, non-Projects task labels, and bottom-status task detail now use Material `MaterialText` typography through those shell component files. Projects page-level Import/New Project actions are gated by `project-actions-visible` from `app.slint`, so only the Dashboard shows them; Projects secondary pages rely on their local `PageHeader` back affordance and page-local actions. Non-Projects main pages receive the HTML reference's page-heading actions through the same `HubPageHeader` action strip: `app.slint` supplies page-specific secondary/primary labels and icons, while `shell_page_components.slint` keeps the shared `HubCommandButton` rendering and click dispatch. `app.slint` now mounts each selected page inside a shared `Fill` slot and gives the page stretch constraints only, keeping page hosting semantic instead of assigning fixed parent width/height on every route.
- `shell_header_components.slint` owns the top-header implementation, frameless-window drag regions, header control-slot centering helper, and `HubTopHeader` orchestration. `HubTopHeader` receives the selected project and active Source Engine context from `app.slint`, showing the selected project in the brand subtitle when available while the imported Source Engine selector stays beside it. `app.slint` forwards all shell chrome metrics from the shared `ResponsiveState`: compact windows use the shorter shell row height, compact shell gaps, compact horizontal padding, and the medium control height for header/page actions. `components.slint` exports `HubTopHeader` from this module, and `ui_shell_composition_contract.rs`, `ui_shell_header_contract.rs`, and `ui_shell_window_contract.rs` check that the top-header body, drag `TouchArea`, responsive metric forwarding, control-slot helper, and popup import remain in the focused component files while the deleted `shell.slint` path stays absent.
- `shell_header_popup_components.slint` owns the top-header Source Engine popup components and user menu popup. `HeaderEngineSelector` keeps only the trigger, anchor `x`/`y`, popup sizing, and callback forwarding; the popup paper and padding come from `HubAnchoredPopupPanel`, the heading/count strip comes from `HubPopupSectionHeader`, and the engine rows/empty state live in `HeaderEngineOptionsList` over `HubMenuListViewport`. `HeaderEngineOption` and `HeaderUserMenuAction` inherit `HubMenuRow`, preserving dense/selected/disabled popup row behavior without local `ListTile`, `InfoRow`, or `ActionRow` shells. `HeaderUserMenu` keeps only trigger and action callback routing; its opened profile row and divider now come from `HubPopupProfileHeader` and `HubPopupDivider`. The popup module is intentionally imported by `shell_header_components.slint` rather than publicly re-exported through `components.slint`, so the public shell surface stays limited to `HubTopHeader`.
- `shell_layout_components.slint` owns the outer CSS-like shell slots that map the HTML reference's `window-view`, `app-bar`, `drawer-shell`, `workspace-surface`, and `page-content-slot` roles into Slint. `HubShellRootSurface` keeps the full-window background/clip surface, `HubShellStack` owns the vertical AppBar/body stack, `HubShellBody` owns the horizontal drawer/workspace flex row, `HubWorkspaceSurface` owns the right-side page-header/content/status column, and `HubPageContentSlot` inherits `Fill` for routed page content. `app.slint` now composes those named slots instead of anonymous `Rectangle` plus local `VerticalLayout`/`HorizontalLayout` containers, so the shell's top/left/right/bottom alignment rules are reusable and contract-checked without moving page data routing out of `HubWindow`.
- `shell_sidebar_components.slint` owns the full left-sidebar implementation. `HubNavSidebar` keeps expanded/collapsed navigation, Material `NavRail` hosting, selected-project/Source Engine state forwarding, and composition of the private `HubSidebarCollapseControl`; that collapse-control primitive forwards the Drawer affordance to `HubSidebarCollapseButton` from `button_components.slint`, where the Material `StateLayerArea`, icon mapping, label collapse, and click forwarding live with the rest of the button family. `app.slint` derives an effective collapsed state from the user's `nav-collapsed` setting plus `ResponsiveState.compact`, so 1024px-class windows automatically use the Material rail even before the user toggles collapse; wider windows still honor the manual expanded/collapsed preference. `NavStatusPanel` keeps the Material `HubPanel`, selected-project label, engine status badge, update row, token-derived height, and Material text bindings. `components.slint` exports `HubNavSidebar` from this module and `HubSidebarCollapseButton` from the button module, and `ui_shell_composition_contract.rs`, `ui_shell_navigation_contract.rs`, and `ui_shell_window_contract.rs` check that sidebar implementation and effective collapsed-state forwarding stay in the focused shell files while the deleted `shell.slint` path stays absent.
- `shell_page_components.slint` owns `HubPageHeader` and `HubStatusBar`. `HubPageHeader` keeps the page title/subtitle band plus a shared two-button action strip. The strip still renders Dashboard-only Import/New Project actions when `projects-action-strip-visible` is true, and otherwise renders the page-level secondary/primary action labels and icons forwarded by `app.slint` for Editor, Builds, Assets, Plugins, Cloud, Team, Learn, and Settings. This makes the native shell match the HTML reference `pageHeading(...)` structure without giving each page a local header-button implementation. `HubStatusBar` keeps selected-project and active Source Engine context badges and remains mounted on the Projects dashboard so that selected-project context stays visible in shell chrome instead of being available only through the user popup. Both receive compact state, responsive page-title/action/status heights, compact page padding, and tokenized context widths from `app.slint` instead of deriving responsive layout from their own resolved width. `components.slint` exports these two components from the page chrome module, and `ui_shell_composition_contract.rs` plus `ui_shell_window_contract.rs` check that they stay in this focused owner while the deleted `shell.slint` path stays absent.
- Projects secondary pages also pass `header-visible: false` into `HubPageHeader`, so the outer Projects title/action band belongs only to the Dashboard. New Project, Project Browser, and Project Detail start from their local `PageHeader` back affordance and page-local controls instead of reserving an empty parent header row.
- `navigation.slint` owns Hub navigation composition. Its collapsed branch wraps the imported Material `NavigationRail`; its expanded branch keeps the wider Hub row-style `NavButton` list, with each row body using Hub token geometry plus a Material `StateLayerArea` and `MaterialText` label.
- `data_display.slint` owns reusable rows, metric cards, `CatalogPage`, catalog list panels, and the generic Material-backed panel scroll viewport. `table_view_components.slint` owns table headers, rows, bodies, and table panel shells; Browser-specific table headers keep their local column model but delegate visible label cells to `TableColumnHeader`. `tree_view_components.slint` owns `TreeItemData`, `HubTreeRow`, and `HubTreeView` so tree behavior can evolve without growing the list/data-row module. Development-only component sample surfaces stay removed; contracts validate real Hub pages and wrappers instead.
- `overlays.slint` owns popup-style reusable surfaces. `HubAnchoredPopupPanel` owns close policy, anchor geometry, panel padding, and panel spacing for topbar popovers; `HubPopupSectionHeader`, `HubPopupProfileHeader`, and `HubPopupDivider` own reusable popover content chrome; `HubSelectMenu` and `HubSelectDropDownSurface` own select/dropdown popup sizing; and drawer/window wrappers stay in the same overlay family.
- `project_dashboard.slint` owns the Projects dashboard composition: toolbar sizing inputs, card-section inputs, lower `PanelGrid` sizing, and callbacks. `project_dashboard_components.slint` owns the exported dashboard-specific components `ProjectCover`, `ProjectCard`, `ProjectFlow`, `DashboardProjectCardsSection`, `DashboardToolbar`, `DashboardQuickActionRow`, `DashboardRecentProjectsPanel`, and `DashboardQuickActionsPanel`, keeping toolbar `ResponsiveSlot` internals, search/filter/sort controls, grid/list mode buttons, card visuals, card scrolling, empty-card state, Show More button, recent-project `DataTable`, quick-action `PanelListViewport`, empty quick-action state, and repeated quick-action `ActionRow` wiring out of the page root. `ProjectDashboardPage` now inherits `PageScrollSurface` directly for page overflow and inherited content-width/viewport-height sizing, while project card titles use Material `MaterialText` typography and dashboard empty states route through shared `EmptyStatePanel`. The lower Recent Projects and Quick Actions panels are typed `PanelSlot`-backed components, so their basis/grow/min-width semantics stay in the page while the Material-backed panel surface, padded body, table/list bodies, and empty states live in the dashboard component module. Quick Actions route repeated rows through the exported `DashboardQuickActionRow` wrapper; the wrapper owns the `ActionRow` action binding and trigger forwarding, while `DashboardQuickActionsPanel` owns the shared scroll viewport and forwards only the dashboard `quick-action(id)` callback. Project card flow keeps tokenized min/preferred card sizing on Slint built-in layout properties; it deliberately does not pass page-derived `content-width` or card-height values through `ProjectFlow` custom properties into the repeated `ProjectCard` delegate, because Slint 1.16.1 can panic in binding analysis for that cross-component repeater path. Repeated card labels (`modified-label`, `pinned-label`, `missing-label`) are projected into `ProjectCardData` by Rust so repeated card delegates do not bind page-level `UiTextData` through custom child properties, another shape the Slint 1.16.1 Rust generator rejects in debug binding analysis. `project_page_components.slint` owns shared Projects secondary-page building blocks; `PageHeader` and `ProjectSettingSummaryRow` use Material `MaterialText` for visible title and label typography, while `project_browser_components.slint` owns Browser-specific select wrappers and `ProjectBrowserRow` title/meta typography. `project_detail_components.slint` owns the Detail-specific action buttons, status strip, information section, and change-engine section. `project_new_page.slint` owns New Project page flow and its settings/summary/template `PanelSlot` call sites, `project_browser_page.slint` owns Browser page flow and the custom inner `ScrollView` row-width viewport, `project_detail_page.slint` owns Project Detail page flow and its detail/action `PanelSlot` call sites, and `project_pages.slint` now only re-exports those three secondary pages. New Project, Browser, and Detail page roots derive content width from `PageScrollSurface`; New Project and Detail standard two-column panels use `PanelSlot` for the Material-backed surface and padded body while preserving their page-level basis/grow/min-width semantics. Project Detail's pending delete state uses the shared `StatusBanner` at error tone inside the Actions panel flow, so the guarded Recycle Bin confirmation remains part of the Material-backed panel composition instead of becoming a page-local loose text block. `projects.slint` remains a subpage router, inherits the shared `Fill` slot behavior instead of binding each subpage to `parent.width`/`parent.height`, and binds New Project to `new-project-location` while Settings keeps the default-project-directory editor on `project-location`. It also remembers the Project Detail entry subpage, so dashboard card/table detail opens return to the dashboard, while Project Browser row detail opens return to the browser selection panel. Dashboard and Browser no longer share the removed `project-entry-mode`, `project-list-rows`, or `recent-project-rows` staging inputs: Dashboard consumes `dashboard-project-rows`, Browser consumes `project-browser-rows`, and View All Projects routes only through the browser subpage.
- Project secondary-page compact page padding/gap state is forwarded from `HubWindow`'s shared `ResponsiveState`: `app.slint` passes `responsive-state.compact` as `viewport-compact`, `projects.slint` forwards that property to New Project, Project Browser, and Project Detail, and those page roots derive their local `compact-page` value from the forwarded state. This keeps global viewport breakpoints centralized in `layout.slint` while still allowing Projects secondary pages to use compact page padding/gap budgets.
- `app.slint` is now only the public `HubWindow` binding surface plus shell/page routing. It delegates chrome to `HubTopHeader`, `HubNavSidebar`, `HubPageHeader`, and `HubStatusBar` instead of directly drawing header/nav/status layout, consumes `ResponsiveState` for viewport breakpoint state, and forwards the selected project, active Source Engine, compact state, and localized shell copy into the top header, nav sidebar, page header, and status bar.

The component-contract milestone treats root/page `.slint` files as structural entrypoints. `app.slint` may own the `HubWindow` property/callback surface and route selected pages, but it must not grow page-specific helper components. Page roots such as `project_dashboard.slint`, `project_new_page.slint`, `project_browser_page.slint`, `project_detail_page.slint`, `editor.slint`, `builds.slint`, `settings.slint`, `cloud.slint`, `team.slint`, and the catalog pages expose one page component each; helper rows, typed panels, card sections, shell fragments, and list bodies belong in their focused `*_components.slint` owner modules. `project_pages.slint` remains a pure aggregate re-export for the three Projects secondary pages, and the old `shell.slint` migration note remains deleted. `ui_foundation_contract.rs` now mechanically checks those entrypoint and component-module ownership rules in addition to the public `components.slint` thin-entrypoint check.
- `editor.slint`, `builds.slint`, `settings.slint`, `cloud.slint`, and `team.slint` use `WorkspacePanelSection` so workspace panel rows share tokenized heights and compact behavior instead of repeating `flow-height` breakpoint formulas in each page. These workspace pages now derive compact thresholds from `PageScrollSurface.content-width`, inherit `PageScrollSurface` directly as their page roots, and let the shared surface plus stretch layout fill rows instead of assigning `width: root.content-width`. `editor.slint`, `builds.slint`, and `settings.slint` route `WorkspacePanelSection` child sizing through `ResponsiveSlot` semantics plus direct paired Slint flex properties at the child use site; Builds, Settings, and Editor's standard workspace panels use `PanelSlot`, which preserves that `ResponsiveSlot` boundary while moving repeated `HubPanel` and padded body layout into `surfaces.slint`. Settings now routes Toolchain, Build Defaults, Default Paths, and Configuration Health through typed `PanelSlot`-backed components in `settings_page_components.slint`; `settings.slint` keeps only the section sizing, flex mirrors, scroll state, data forwarding, and browse/save callback forwarding at those use sites. Editor keeps one explicit `ResponsiveSlot` for the split source-engine/build-history side column because that slot contains two stacked nested panels rather than one standard panel body. Editor, Builds, and the Settings detail row now give their main slots a larger token basis with grow weight 2 and their side slots a side-panel token basis with grow weight 1, so pages no longer precompute overview/actions/config/detail widths from a remaining-width formula. Settings default-path rows keep a token preferred width and stretch inside `PanelListViewport` instead of deriving row width from the panel's resolved width. `project_dashboard.slint`, `project_new_page.slint`, `project_browser_page.slint`, and `project_detail_page.slint` now inherit `PageScrollSurface` directly; Projects toolbar slots still use `ResponsiveSlot`, while New Project and Detail standard panel columns use `PanelSlot` so their Material-backed surfaces share the same shell as workspace panels. Browser keeps a separate `browser-scroll-y` and explicit list panel for its inner Material list viewport, so list scrolling does not reuse the inherited page-scroll state and row width can follow the visible viewport. Dashboard toolbar selects, lower Recent/Quick panels, token card basis, New/Detail main-side panel bases, and Browser toolbar select bases now avoid width-minus-gap formulas, while dashboard repeat cards use preferred/min widths because Slint 1.16.1 rejects `ResponsiveSlot` wrappers and page-derived sizing properties inside that repeated card path. `builds.slint` consumes selected project detail so build/package rows describe the current project context instead of only the active engine, sizes its summary row token for five Build/Open/Launch/Package/Install action rows, uses `PanelSlot` for the five source/control/pipeline/status-history/operation-timeline panels, and uses Material `MaterialText` for the current-task status headline. `cloud.slint` sets `compact-stack: false`, drives `compact-rows` for wrapped metric cards, routes metric card basis/min-width/grow through tokenized `ResponsiveSlot` inputs, and uses four-column/two-column/one-column Taffy growth instead of page-local content-width-minus-gap division for multi-column metric rows. Shared `CatalogPage`, Cloud service rows, and Team member rows consume `PageScrollSurface.content-height` for their list panel viewport budget rather than recomputing `root.height - page-padding - bottom-status` in page code. `team.slint` keeps member empty states on `EmptyStatePanel`, while the other workspace pages use the default compact stacking behavior.

Cloud and Team top summary cards now share `OverviewPanel` from `surfaces.slint`, while Cloud metric cards and Team summary cards share `MetricCard` from `data_display.slint`. Those pages provide title/status and label/primary/secondary values plus responsive slot sizing instead of owning panel chrome. Cloud and Team lower lists now route through typed `PanelSlot` components: `CloudServicesPanel` owns the Services header, badge, `PanelListViewport`, repeated `CloudServiceRow` bindings, and service empty state in `cloud_page_components.slint`; `TeamMembersPanel` owns the Members header, badge, `PanelListViewport`, repeated `TeamMemberRow` bindings, and no-members empty state in `team_page_components.slint`. The page roots keep only list height, row-height where needed, data/copy forwarding, scroll binding, and compact-label state. A generic `ListPanel` shell was removed because Slint 1.16.1 binding analysis panics when page-authored dynamic row children are forwarded through that reusable child slot.

The UI contract now blocks the specific unsafe dashboard card path as well: `ProjectFlow` must not reintroduce `available-content`, `card-height-token`, or dashboard card-height/basis properties sourced from page geometry and then consumed by the repeated card delegate. Card flow responsiveness stays with tokenized min/preferred widths plus the surrounding `Flow` layout.

The historical `hub-responsive-baseline` milestone recorded the standalone launcher boundary before the Tauri cutover. The current boundary is different: `zircon_hub` owns a Tauri v2 desktop shell, `build.rs` delegates to `tauri_build`, and `tauri.conf.json` points the window at the Vite React frontend. Editor integration remains process-oriented: `EditorLaunchCommand` builds `--project` and `--create-project` command lines and `launch_editor` spawns `zircon_editor` as a child process. Hub runtime code may orchestrate configs, recent projects, source engines, builds, package/install actions, and child-process status, but it must not register runtime lifecycle modules, dynamically load runtime libraries, or route Hub UI ownership into editor-owned paths. `ui_foundation_contract.rs` now locks that boundary together with the React/MUI component-system foundation contracts.

## Runtime-State Data Flow

The runtime-state integration split is the UI data contract for selected-project and Source Engine scope: `HubRuntime -> HubSnapshot -> HubScope -> view-model builders -> binding::apply_snapshot() -> Slint DTO props`. Slint pages consume projected DTOs; they may forward `ProjectDetailData`, `QuickActionData`, `SourceEngineData`, `WorkspaceActionReadinessData`, catalog row data, and `ActionRecordData`, but they must not rebuild selected-project, latest-recent, or active-engine fallback rules.

`shared.slint` remains the DTO boundary. It can expose fields such as `has-selected-project`, `selected-project-label`, `source-engine-label`, `can-build`, disabled-reason copy, and row source labels. It cannot introduce callback-local filesystem scanning or selected-project lookup. Page roots and component wrappers forward those fields into shared wrappers such as `DashboardQuickActionsPanel`, `BuildControlsPanel`, `CatalogPage`, `CloudPackageActionsPanel`, `TeamMembersPanel`, `ProjectDetailPage`, and `OperationTimelinePanel`.

Runtime owners for the flow are intentionally narrow. `HubSnapshot::scope()` resolves project and Source Engine state; `src/tauri_app/runtime_state/scoped_views.rs` owns Assets/Plugins/Learn/Team catalog roots; command handlers record success/failure through `record_action_and_persist()`; and restart-visible state lives in `HubConfig.runtime`. Dashboard Quick Actions are the only visible surface that may use latest-recent fallback when no selected project exists. Builds, Project Detail, catalog pages, and source-engine scoped panels consume selected-project or active-engine DTO copy from the snapshot instead of querying config or recent lists directly.

The consolidation gate for this data flow is `hub-runtime-state-integration-contract-docs`. It ties `ui_selected_project_runtime_contract.rs`, `ui_selected_project_catalog_contract.rs`, `project_quick_actions_contract.rs`, `project_workflow_contract.rs`, `project_source_engine_contract.rs`, and `project_path_scope_contract.rs` together so the responsive component system can stay visual/layout-focused while the Rust contracts protect scope ownership.

## Docs/Contracts Refresh Gate

The docs refresh gate treats the component stack as a contract matrix: `tokens -> layout primitives -> surfaces -> inputs/navigation/data display/overlays -> shell and page-specific wrappers -> page roots`. Future edits should update the owner module and its focused contract instead of adding root-page exceptions or another broad scanner. `hub_docs_contract.rs` keeps this document, the Hub index, state foundations, project lifecycle, actionable pages, and Settings status docs aligned with that matrix.

The current static contract split is intentional. `ui_foundation_contract.rs` owns thin entrypoints and component-module ownership, `ui_global_rules_contract.rs` owns no raw text/icons/touch/percent/page-geometry drift, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs` owns Material-template consumption, `ui_taffy_layout_contract.rs` owns Taffy/flex pairing, `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs` owns workspace section layout, `ui_project_layout_contract.rs` owns Projects layout, `ui_selected_project_runtime_contract.rs` and `ui_selected_project_catalog_contract.rs` own selected-project runtime/catalog scope, `ui_visual_standard_contract.rs` owns visual artifact handoff, and `hub_docs_contract.rs` owns this documentation matrix.

The known Slint 1.16.1 constraints remain part of the design: repeated card delegates avoid page-derived custom geometry, root pages inherit `PageScrollSurface` or `CatalogPage` directly, direct `flex-*` properties stay beside matching semantic slot inputs, and generic dynamic child wrappers are avoided where Slint binding analysis panics. These constraints are documented so later acceptance work can verify screenshots and generated UI without "simplifying" back to page-local layout formulas.

## Layout Contract

Pages should describe semantic regions instead of pixel-placement details: shell, header, nav rail, toolbar, card flow, panel grid, list/table, detail, and action regions. Page-level code may choose `min-width`, `preferred-width`, row-height parameters, and breakpoint tokens, while workspace rows, Projects toolbar items, and Projects secondary-page columns should express Taffy basis/grow/shrink through `ResponsiveSlot` or `PanelSlot` semantic inputs, then mirror those inputs immediately to Slint `flex-*` properties at the direct Flexbox child use site. It should not place business regions with `x`/`y`, nor introduce literal page breakpoints such as `root.width < 980px`.

Workspace-style pages should use `WorkspacePanelSection` for repeated two-panel rows and metric groups that need shared height math. The primitive is still Slint-native `FlexboxLayout`, but it centralizes compact height calculation, exposes `compact-stack` for rows that must wrap instead of stack, exposes `compact-rows` for wrapped metric groups that need more than two rows, and keeps row heights in `HubTokens`. It also exposes `section-padding*` inputs; those insets are included in both compact and regular section-height calculations before being forwarded to the internal `FlexboxLayout`, so a page that needs extra row breathing room does not have to wrap a section in another `Rectangle` or duplicate `FlexboxLayout` padding. Workspace pages derive their semantic content width from the surrounding `PageScrollSurface`, while `WorkspacePanelSection` itself keeps `preferred-width: 0px` so it does not derive preferred width from its own layout result. The migrated workspace page roots inherit `PageScrollSurface` directly and do not rebind a nested page child to `root.width` or `root.height`. This makes `editor`, `builds`, `settings`, `cloud`, and `team` easier to scan and keeps future breakpoint changes in one place. The shell lets Slint/Taffy allocate the page host as the horizontally and vertically stretched sibling after the navigation rail and lets the main body stretch below the top header; `app.slint` must not hand-write `parent.width - root.nav-width` or `root.height - root.shell-header-height` content formulas, because those formulas create layout-info dependency loops when the shell, page header, and routed page all query each other.

Percent sizing is intentionally avoided in Hub-owned Slint files. Shared stretch components such as `InfoRow`, `ActionRow`, `PanelGrid`, and `PanelHeader` use `horizontal-stretch`, `min-width`, parent/content-width inputs, and `preferred-width: 0px` instead of `preferred-width: 100%`, so the component does not ask Slint to derive its preferred size from the same layout result it participates in.

Fixed sizes remain allowed at the atom layer where they carry real interaction meaning: icon sizes, button/control heights, Material text-field height, table/list row heights, 1px borders/dividers, popup anchors, and window drag/resize math. Those values should come from `HubTokens` whenever they are part of the common Hub scale, and the foundation contract locks the token formulas so page files cannot drift back to local `34px`/`38px`/`42px` controls, `14px`/`10px` gaps, or ad hoc list/table row heights.

Visible Hub typography follows the same component contract. Page and component files should use Material `MaterialText` or Hub wrappers such as `FieldLabel` and `MutedText` instead of raw Slint `Text`, `inherits Text`, or direct `font-size`/`font-weight` bindings. Read-only `MaterialTypography.*.font_size` references remain allowed when a layout needs to derive row or section height from the template's text metrics.

Icon-like affordances follow the same rule. Toolbar, row, status, popup, and window controls should use bundled SVG assets or Material icon slots instead of character literals such as `+`, `>`, `v`, `!`, `?`, `[]`, `::`, `==`, or `...` in `text` and `fallback-text` bindings.

Pointer interaction follows the imported Material/template path. Buttons, list rows, cards, toolbars, selectors, and row action zones should use Material button primitives, `ListTile`, `PopupMenu`, or `StateLayerArea`; direct Slint `TouchArea` usage is reserved for the frameless shell drag region in `shell_header_components.slint`.

The current responsive breakpoints are:

- compact: widths below `HubTokens.breakpoint-compact` (`1040px`)
- medium: widths below `HubTokens.breakpoint-medium` (`1280px`)
- wide: widths at or above `HubTokens.breakpoint-wide` (`1536px`)

`layout.slint` now owns both window-wide and content-width responsive predicates. `ResponsiveState` remains the only place that evaluates viewport-level compact/medium/wide/short flags from `root.width` and `root.height`, while the zero-size `ResponsiveCollapse` helper evaluates a caller-provided `content-width` against a tokenized collapse threshold. Catalog-style pages and lower workspace lists use that helper for trailing-label collapse instead of repeating `root.content-width < HubTokens.breakpoint-medium` at each row call site. This keeps label-collapse behavior on the same shared responsive primitive as shell breakpoints while still allowing page-specific flow decisions such as Cloud metric row count or Projects column stacking to stay content-relative.

The shell treats widths below the wide breakpoint as compact for the top title bar. At 1280px, the title bar has enough room for the brand, source-engine selector, user controls, and window buttons, but not enough room for the full task/success/warning/error status-pill group without overlap. `app.slint` therefore passes `compact: true` to `HubTopHeader` below `HubTokens.breakpoint-wide`; the status pills remain visible at the 1600px reference width and are hidden at 1280px and 1024px so the chrome stays aligned while page bodies enter their own responsive layouts.

The shell also treats heights below `HubTokens.breakpoint-short` as compact for the left rail. The threshold is intentionally above the 900px and 768px validation sizes so expanded Material `ListTile` navigation rows have enough vertical room; the lower engine/project status card and collapse action are hidden before they can compress the navigation list into the bottom status bar. When visible, the lower card uses token-derived shell row height to reserve room for both active Source Engine status and current selected-project context.

The page header and bottom status bar treat widths below `HubTokens.breakpoint-medium` as compact, but that breakpoint is evaluated by `ResponsiveState` and passed into the shell components from `app.slint`. At wide sizes `HubPageHeader` places selected-project plus active Source Engine badges beside non-Projects page title actions, while `HubStatusBar` keeps the current task text flexible through Material `MaterialText` and places the same badges after it using `HubTokens.status-badge-width`; at compact sizes those badges are hidden, the page header uses the base shell-header height, the status bar uses the medium control height, and the page title padding switches to `HubTokens.page-padding-compact` so the title band and task detail can elide cleanly instead of overlapping shell chrome. These shell components must not calculate compact state, page padding, action height, status height, or badge width from their own resolved width because those values participate in Slint layout info. `app.slint` also derives `header-tight`, `header-minimal`, compact shell row height, compact shell gap/padding, and automatic compact navigation collapse from `ResponsiveState`; the header no longer instantiates its own `ResponsiveState` or derives breakpoints from its resolved width. It still sizes the Source Engine selector from `nav-width`, header button size, and token clamps instead of dividing `root.width`, so compact chrome width remains a shell input contract rather than page-local geometry math.

Projects secondary pages use content-relative breakpoints rather than a single shell width. `ProjectNewPage` keeps project name, location, Source Engine choice, create action, and summary in the main panel, with templates in a side rail only when inherited `PageScrollSurface.content-width` can hold both columns; both panels use `PanelSlot`/`ResponsiveSlot` semantics as direct children of the page `FlexboxLayout` and mirror those semantics to paired Slint flex properties, and the page root inherits the shared Material-backed `PageScrollSurface`. Its main/side columns use tokenized panel bases and minimums instead of subtracting gaps from `content-width`, and its form flow height consumes inherited `PageScrollSurface.content-height` instead of subtracting page padding from the viewport in page code. At 1024px, that rail wraps below the settings panel and remains reachable through the page scroll. `ProjectBrowserPage` wraps search, filter, and sort controls into a second toolbar row when the content width cannot hold the wide toolbar, with each toolbar control sized through `ResponsiveSlot` and paired Slint flex properties; filter and sort selects use a shared toolbar-select basis/grow path rather than a half-width subtraction, and the browser page uses a separate inner Material `ScrollView` only for list rows with `browser-scroll-y` while the list panel budget comes from inherited `PageScrollSurface.content-height`. `ProjectDetailPage` uses the same content-driven flow: the main cover/info panel remains first, the Actions/Bound Source Engine panel stacks below it at 1024px, both detail columns use tokenized `PanelSlot`/`ResponsiveSlot` bases with paired Slint flex properties, and the detail page inherits `PageScrollSurface` directly so `content-height` drives the detail/action panel budget while short windows scroll instead of clipping project-info rows. Workspace pages use `WorkspacePanelSection` for wrapped summary/metric rows; Builds uses the section's compact height override so its Source Build summary can stay content-sized while the selected-project actions panel keeps the full five-action list height, Editor uses the same semantic row-budget pattern for its active-engine overview plus action row, and Cloud passes a compact row count plus tokenized metric slot basis/min-width/grow for the four-card metric set. Cloud keeps two-column cards on the same minimum card width used by four-column mode and lets Taffy grow them across the row, so one-column, two-column, and wide four-column layouts expand by row token and Taffy growth instead of relying on page-local height, half-width formulas, or a medium-panel basis that would force compact layouts back to one column.

## Validation
