Learn now follows the same selected-project snapshot contract as Assets, Plugins, and Team: project selection refreshes selected-project documentation before projection, while Source Engine changes still rebuild the source-scoped documentation set. The Learn row metadata carries Selected Project or Source Engine source labels, and the page title plus empty-state detail switch based on whether a project is selected.

Quick-action fallback is part of the responsive workflow contract. When Package, Install, or Open Editor promotes the latest recent project because no project is selected, the Tauri runtime refreshes selected-project scoped Assets, Plugins, and Team data before projecting the next Hub snapshot; if that action activates a different bound Source Engine, it refreshes the full Assets/Learn/Plugins/Team source-scoped set. If `selected_project_path` is set but no longer resolves to a recent project, Quick Action projection and runtime helpers show selected-project-unavailable copy and treat it as no usable project target instead of promoting the newest recent project. Build can use the same selected/newest-recent target, but it is enabled only when that project has a Hub metadata binding to an installed Source Engine. Build target copy distinguishes a project with no binding from a stale binding whose Source Engine record is unavailable. Normal project selection uses the stricter selected-project path: when Hub metadata binds that project to a Source Engine, selection makes the bound engine current and refreshes project/engine scoped views before Builds, Cloud, Assets, Plugins, Team, Learn, or shell context render again. New Project uses the same engine-validity rule: create readiness requires the selected Source Engine id to resolve to an installed engine, runtime creation revalidates the id before activating the engine or launching the Editor, and the default engine tracks the active Source context only when the current default is empty, invalid, or was following the previous active engine. A valid explicit New Project engine choice remains stable across unrelated active-engine switches. Existing-project open only remembers the recent/selected project path; it does not write the current active Source Engine into Hub metadata, so unbound and stale binding states remain visible until New Project creation or an explicit Project Detail Source Engine choice updates them. The opened/created project remember path uses the same bound-engine activation before persisting the new `last_project_path`, so newly created project bindings are current in the next projected snapshot. Startup uses the Editor session's `last_project_path` only when it still matches a merged recent project, then restores `selected_project_path` before the first scoped catalogs are refreshed. Runtime selection, Quick Action target resolution, Quick Action visible target labels, Project Detail projection, remove-from-Hub, pending-delete cleanup, and selected-project scoped refresh change detection all use the shared `project_paths_match` rule, which reuses Hub project metadata key normalization instead of direct `PathBuf` equality and treats Windows drive-letter paths case-insensitively even when those persisted strings are validated on non-Windows hosts. Assets, Plugins, Learn, and Team scanner de-duplication use `project_filesystem_path_key`, which canonicalizes existing roots/manifests and then applies the same metadata-key rule; Source Engine registration now hashes and de-duplicates source checkout paths through that same filesystem key before migrating existing engine metadata. Startup, settings save, Source Engine selection/removal, build startup after registering settings, project actions that activate a bound engine, and Source/output folder browsing all call `src/tauri_app/runtime_state/scoped_views.rs` for the full Assets/Learn/Plugins/Team refresh set, so page data follows one source-scoped refresh contract instead of callback-local four-call chains. Team Git discovery now has behavior tests that create local project and Source Engine repositories: `scoped_views.rs` proves the selected project repository is preferred and the Source Engine repository is used when no project is selected. Project card, browser row, and detail projections also use only that per-project metadata for the visible engine/version binding; an unbound project stays localized as `Unbound`/`No Source Engine` even when Hub has another active Source Engine, while a stale stored engine id stays visible as metadata but renders localized `Source Engine unavailable`/`Unavailable` instead of a valid engine version. Project Detail status is localized as `Missing`, `Ready`, or `Invalid` from the same Rust projection before Builds and Projects pages render it. Builds page Build, Open Editor, Package, and Install rows route through selected-project-only callbacks that reuse the same build/package/install helpers without allowing the Dashboard quick-action fallback; Open Editor, Package, and Install stay disabled until `ProjectDetailData` has an actual selected project, while Build consumes the separate row/detail `can_build` flag so an openable but unbound or stale-bound project can still be opened, packaged, or installed without showing Build as available. Runtime also re-checks the valid bound Source Engine before building and refreshes source-scoped page data before projecting the building snapshot, so the Builds workflow cannot silently build or display stale context from the previously active engine. Removing or confirming deletion of the selected project clears those scoped views through the same refresh path; the shared remove helper also clears pending-delete state for the removed path after normalized matching. Confirm Delete waits for the Windows Recycle Bin command to finish successfully before removing Hub recent and project metadata, so a PowerShell/FileSystem deletion failure leaves the Hub entry intact and reports the command error instead of silently removing the project from Hub. Project-scoped actions also activate the project's bound Source Engine when Hub metadata has one, while `ProjectDetailData` remains an explicit no-selection row until `selected_project_path` is set.

Plugins follows that same scoped snapshot rule: selected-project plugin manifests keep Project scope, engine manifests keep Source Engine scope, and the final `PluginData` projection repeats the Project-before-Engine ordering before rows reach Slint. Plugin manifest de-duplication now uses the same canonicalized filesystem path key as selected-project asset roots and Team Git root discovery, so scoped catalog rows keep one project/engine classification even when the same root is reached through an alias.

Learn follows the scoped catalog rule as documentation instead of manifests: selected-project docs are discovered and projected before Source Engine docs, duplicate docs roots are removed through `project_filesystem_path_key`, and Slint receives selected-project empty-state copy plus per-row source metadata.

Builds page project actions are deliberately stricter than Dashboard Quick Actions, and the page subtitle plus visible actions-panel title use selected-project wording so the page context matches those callbacks. The Builds callbacks use selected-project-only runtime entrypoints for Build, Open Editor, Package, and Install, so a disabled or stale Builds row cannot silently promote the newest recent project or build whatever Source Engine happened to be active. Dashboard Quick Actions remain the only place where no-selection fallback to the newest recent project is part of the visible contract, and Build still requires that fallback target to have a valid bound Source Engine before it can run; a stale selected project path suppresses that fallback instead of targeting another project. Runtime errors call out missing bindings and unavailable bound-engine records separately, and the action-history layer persists those package/install selection failures as failed recoverable records instead of only surfacing transient bottom-bar status. Builds source summary and source history follow the same workflow scope by resolving the selected project's bound Source Engine first, using active-engine fallback only when no project is selected, and suppressing fallback for explicitly selected unbound or stale-bound projects; the no-history copy names selected-project or active Source Engine context instead of the old active-engine-only wording. Build history rows now receive a bounded build-log excerpt plus the exact `tools/zircon_build.py` command line from the Rust build runner, while the persistent Hub action history records build/open/package/install outcomes, recovery hints, output directories, and Editor child-process ids. Project create/open launch successes are recorded after recent-project metadata persistence, so the UI will not reload with a successful launch history row when the project state write failed. The compact Builds summary row also keeps a `controls-first` height check so short stacked layouts put selected-project actions before the Source Build summary through paired `order`/`flex-order` values while preserving the same Taffy slot sizing.

Focused companion tests such as `zircon_hub/tests/ui_selected_project_catalog_contract.rs`, `zircon_hub/tests/ui_selected_project_runtime_contract.rs`, `zircon_hub/tests/ui_project_scope_contract.rs`, `zircon_hub/tests/ui_project_navigation_contract.rs`, and `zircon_hub/tests/ui_panel_slot_contract.rs` lock the important structural rules as integration tests so the file scanner does not force the full Slint-generated Hub library into a Rust unit-test harness: the `components.slint` entrypoint must stay thin, the direct `@material` bridge must stay available, `Page` must use Material `ScrollView`, `Divider` must use Material `HorizontalDivider`/`VerticalDivider`, data-display table/list surfaces and Projects dashboard/secondary scroll surfaces must use Material `ScrollView`, data-display table headers, `ProjectTableRow` cells, `CatalogListPanel` empty-state titles, Projects dashboard card/empty titles, Projects secondary-page shared header/summary/browser labels, Projects page section/status labels, Builds current-task status headline, Cloud metric status labels, and Team summary/empty-state titles must use Material `MaterialText`, the Hub window must install `ZirconMaterialTheme`, `HubPanel` must remain backed by Material card surfaces without placing a panel-level `TouchArea` above `@children`, `PanelSlot` must inherit `ResponsiveSlot`, re-export from `components.slint`, and own the standard Material panel body wrapper, `PanelHeader` right actions must stay on Material `OutlineButton`, Hub `Badge`/`StatusBadge` labels plus `PanelHeader`, `StatusBanner`, and shared `FieldLabel`/`MutedText` title wrappers must remain backed by Material `MaterialText` without returning to raw `Text` font bindings, and every Hub UI `.slint` file is globally scanned so raw `Text`, `inherits Text`, or direct `font-size`/`font-weight` bindings cannot return outside those focused component checks. The same global scan blocks character-icon `text`/`fallback-text` literals for icon-like symbols so controls keep using SVG assets or Material icon slots, reserves direct `TouchArea` for only the shell window-drag layer, and forbids percent sizing in width/height/preferred/min/max bindings. Hub `PillButton`/`IconButton` must remain backed by Material button primitives without local `TouchArea` pointer layers, header `StatusPill` must remain backed by Material `ActionChip`, Hub form pages must use the Material-backed `HubTextField` wrapper instead of `LineEdit`, Hub `SearchBox` must keep the reference Hub outlined shell with compact `TextInput` instead of returning to the earlier Material `SearchBar` capsule branch, collapsed Hub navigation must remain backed by Material `NavigationRail`, expanded Hub `NavButton` rows must stay in `navigation.slint` with Material `StateLayerArea` and `MaterialText`, Hub `InfoRow`, `ActionRow`, shared `BuildHistoryRow`, Editor source-engine rows, and source-engine popup option rows must remain backed by Material `ListTile`, dashboard `ProjectCard`, dashboard `ProjectTableRow`, `ProjectBrowserRow`, and the sidebar collapse control must use Material `StateLayerArea` instead of custom card/row/thumbnail/body/collapse hit targets for hover/press feedback, while `ProjectBrowserRow` keeps row selection and the trailing detail transition in separate tokenized Material `StateLayerArea` zones with a centered SVG detail icon and a fallback branch for captured trailing clicks. Hub `SegmentButton` must remain backed by Material `SegmentedButton`, Hub `ToolbarSelect` must remain backed by Material `OutlineButton` and `PopupMenu`, Hub `DropDownButton` must remain backed by Material `OutlineButton`/`TonalButton`, Projects engine/template choice rows must remain backed by Material `ListTile`, `HubPageHeader` and `HubStatusBar` must receive selected-project and active Source Engine context from `HubWindow` and hide those badges on compact widths, Builds must receive selected project detail and selected-project build-context source history rows, Assets must prioritize selected-project assets and pass selected-project empty-state copy into the page, `ProjectDetailData` must remain an explicit no-selection state until `selected_project_path` resolves, selected-project matching must reuse `project_paths_match` and the metadata-key normalization, Plugins must surface selected-project/Source Engine scope and pass selected-project empty-state copy into the page, Team must pass selected-project empty-state copy into its local Git view, Quick Actions must derive their selected/latest project targeting from `HubSnapshot`, the component modules and tokens must exist, workspace pages must route panel rows through `WorkspacePanelSection`, Editor/Settings/Builds workspace rows plus Projects dashboard and secondary pages must keep direct `flex-basis`/`flex-grow`/`flex-shrink`/`flex-order` paired immediately after the matching semantic `basis`/`grow`/`shrink`/`order` lines, viewport breakpoint comparisons must stay centralized in `layout.slint` `ResponsiveState`, business pages may not reintroduce `x`/`y` layout positioning, the app shell may not return to hand-written remaining-width subtraction, and Hub UI files may not introduce raw pixel literals above `1px` outside the `MaterialStyleMetrics`/`HubTokens` scale. The global-rules companion also blocks nested `page-surface := PageScrollSurface` wrappers and page-local forwarding of `page-surface.content-width`, `page-surface.content-height`, or `page-surface.viewport-height`, so migrated page roots must inherit `PageScrollSurface` or `CatalogPage` directly. The focused companions also assert that the removed `ButtonStates` development surface does not return, `ComponentSamples` stays internal-only for Material/Taffy coverage, and the old `PlaceholderPage` file stays removed so user-facing routes cannot fall back to a reserved placeholder card. `zircon_hub/Cargo.toml` sets the library target's default unit-test harness to `test = false`; Hub package tests still run through integration tests, while normal `cargo check`/`cargo build` continue compiling the Slint UI library and binary. Compile and visual validation still remain required because Slint layout behavior is ultimately checked through generated UI and real Hub screenshots.

The global guardrail subset now lives in `zircon_hub/tests/ui_global_rules_contract.rs`, covering the full-file scans for MaterialText routing, character-icon literals, direct `TouchArea`, percentage sizing, page-local root-width arithmetic, nested page surfaces, scattered viewport breakpoints, business-page `x`/`y`, and raw `px` layout literals. The Material-usage subset lives in `zircon_hub/tests/ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, covering StatusPill ActionChip delegation, source-engine and Projects choice `ListTile` rows, Material `ScrollView` page/list surfaces, dashboard `StateLayerArea` selectors, and the Projects dashboard router. The Taffy layout subset lives in `zircon_hub/tests/ui_taffy_layout_contract.rs`, covering `WorkspacePanelSection`, `ResponsiveSlot`/`PanelSlot` semantic flex pairing, and real Hub surface consumption of the Material/Taffy wrappers. The selected-project catalog subset lives in `zircon_hub/tests/ui_selected_project_catalog_contract.rs`, covering Assets/Plugins/Learn/Team/Cloud scoped page copy and catalog projection ordering. The selected-project runtime subset lives in `zircon_hub/tests/ui_selected_project_runtime_contract.rs`, covering Builds, Quick Actions, project-detail, path matching, startup, package/install, and delete scoped runtime invariants. The remaining project-scope subset lives in `zircon_hub/tests/ui_project_scope_contract.rs`, covering Cloud package manifest scoping plus dashboard card label projection. The Projects navigation subset lives in `zircon_hub/tests/ui_project_navigation_contract.rs`, covering dashboard/import/browser/detail routing and New Project location-state invariants. The data-display subset lives in `zircon_hub/tests/ui_data_display_contract.rs`, covering Material `ScrollView` usage, Dashboard `DataTable` consumption of `HubTableBody`, table/list/catelog empty-state typography, shared `InfoRow`/`ActionRow` `ListTile` backing, and the shared build-history row path used by Editor and Builds. The inputs subset lives in `zircon_hub/tests/ui_inputs_contract.rs`, covering `PillButton`, `IconButton`, `WindowButton`, `HubTextField`, `SearchBox`, `ToolbarSelect`, `DropDownButton`, `SegmentButton`, and the Projects filter/sort select wrappers. The navigation subset lives in `zircon_hub/tests/ui_navigation_contract.rs`, covering expanded `NavButton` ownership in `navigation.slint` with Material `StateLayerArea`/`MaterialText` and collapsed `NavRail` Material `NavigationRail` adapter wiring through `app.slint`, `shell_sidebar_components.slint`, binding, and view-model projection. The typography subset lives in `zircon_hub/tests/ui_typography_contract.rs`, covering shared text wrappers, Builds current-task status, Cloud/Team metric and empty-state text, dashboard project/empty titles, and Projects workflow page/component labels. The shell-specific checks live in `zircon_hub/tests/ui_shell_header_contract.rs`, `zircon_hub/tests/ui_shell_composition_contract.rs`, `zircon_hub/tests/ui_shell_navigation_contract.rs`, and `zircon_hub/tests/ui_shell_window_contract.rs`, covering `HubTopHeader`, `HubNavSidebar`, `NavStatusPanel`, shell routing from `app.slint`, sidebar collapse state layers, preferred/minimum window sizing, and the selected-page `Fill` host. The retired `ui_contract.rs` and `ui_shell_contract.rs` entrypoints now stay as discoverability stubs, while focused companion contracts own component-family and workflow checks by responsibility.

The same structural contract now requires Project Detail's pending-delete confirmation to render as a shared `StatusBanner` with error tone and the localized confirm/delete detail copy before the confirm/cancel actions. `StatusBanner` keeps a token-derived row height and zero vertical stretch so wide action columns do not expand the warning into a blank card. While that confirmation is pending, the normal Open, pin, remove-from-Hub, delete, and Change Source Engine controls stay hidden so compact viewports show a complete confirmation cluster instead of clipping the lower action rows. This keeps the destructive-action warning on the same Material typography and panel surface path as other Hub status content.

The same contract now requires Cloud and Team to reuse `MetricCard` for metric/summary cards while keeping their typed row viewport on the shared `PanelListViewport` primitive. It forbids `CloudMetric`, `TeamSummaryItem`, and `TeamSummaryCard` from returning as page-local card components.

The shell context contracts in `zircon_hub/tests/ui_shell_header_contract.rs`, `zircon_hub/tests/ui_shell_composition_contract.rs`, and `zircon_hub/tests/ui_shell_navigation_contract.rs` cover `HubTopHeader`, `NavStatusPanel`, and `HubNavSidebar`: `app.slint` must pass `ProjectDetailData`, `SourceEngineData`, and `UiTextData` into the exported shell components, the top-header brand subtitle must derive from the selected project when one is selected and fall back to `UiTextData.game-engine` in the empty-selection dashboard state, and the expanded lower sidebar status card must live in `NavStatusPanel` inside `shell_sidebar_components.slint`. `HubNavSidebar` now only decides when that status panel is visible and forwards semantic project/source-engine/task state; the panel itself owns the Material `MaterialText`, selected-project-only `Badge`, refresh icon row, and token-derived height so sidebar layout does not accumulate another inline card body or show a synthetic "no project" badge. The Slint `UiTextData.current-project` field name remains stable for those shell components, but Rust localization initializes it as `Selected Project` / `选中项目` so visible chrome follows the selected-project workflow when a project badge is actually present instead of implying a separate current-project concept.

`ToolbarSelect` popup behavior is also part of the static contract: the wrapper root must stay unclipped, the Material `PopupMenu` must use a tokenized minimum width, and its offset anchor must keep the menu visible under compact filter/sort toolbar buttons. `HubSelectTrigger` now owns the Material `OutlineButton` hit target, visible select shell, token-sized chevron, state layer, enabled/focused styling, and dense-label mode so toolbar filter/sort controls and the top Source Engine selector share the same anchor geometry instead of repeating local select-trigger paint.

The status-bar contract also forbids deriving compact state or badge width from `HubStatusBar`'s own resolved width, because that creates a Slint layout-info binding loop. The selected-page host contract similarly requires every `app.slint` route to fill the shared `Fill` slot with stretch constraints and forbids returning to per-route `width: parent.width` / `height: parent.height` bindings.

The Projects height contract now treats inherited `PageScrollSurface.content-height` as the source for dashboard lower panel budgets plus New Project, Browser, and Detail row-height ratios. `ProjectDashboardPage`, `ProjectNewPage`, `ProjectBrowserPage`, and `ProjectDetailPage` consume inherited `content-height` directly; dashboard lower-grid fallback height includes `root.content-height / 18` so it remains tied to the shared page surface instead of the surrounding viewport. Projects pages should not return to page-root `root.height / ...` or `root.viewport-height` formulas for those vertical budgets.

The Cloud tab is surfaced as local package/install tooling in navigation copy. Runtime view-model copy, page subtitles, and contract checks forbid account-service wording so responsive shell context does not imply an online Cloud account surface.

The Projects ratio contract keeps proportional toolbar, card, panel, and stacked-detail ratios in `HubTokens` instead of writing numeric `content-width` or `content-height` fractions directly in page files. Dashboard search/select/card/table/lower-panel ratios, Browser toolbar search/select ratios, New Project stack ratios, and Detail cover/badge/main-side/action stack ratios are now named tokens, so page code reads as semantic layout intent instead of a local set of percent formulas. Dashboard project cards now derive their basis from `ProjectFlow.flow-width` plus the `project-dashboard-card-ratio` token, so the row follows its actual Flow container instead of fixed column counts or page-root width arithmetic.

The selected-project workflow contract also covers Builds copy. Build history projection already chooses the selected project's bound Source Engine before falling back to the active engine when no project is selected, and the localized no-history text now says selected project or active Source Engine instead of naming only the active source engine.

The selected-project workflow contract also covers Assets route copy. The catalog already scopes selected-project assets ahead of recent-project and Source Engine asset groups, and the `HubPage::Assets` fallback subtitle plus localized page subtitle now describe selected-project and Source Engine assets rather than a future integration placeholder or vague discovered-assets bucket. The Assets empty-state title and details are also scope-neutral, saying checked project folders plus Source Engine asset roots were empty and explaining selected-project/recent-project/Source Engine roots without returning to active-checkout wording.

Plugins follows the same route-copy rule: selected-project `plugin.toml` manifests are projected before Source Engine plugin manifests, and `HubPage::Plugins` now describes those two scopes directly instead of using generic plugin-discovery/project-extension wording. The Plugins empty-state title and details mirror the same scope-neutral rule by naming checked project folders plus Source Engine plugin roots, or Source Engine plugin roots plus repository fallback roots, instead of only the active source checkout/tree.

The same selected-project workflow contract now includes Cloud: when a project is selected, the Cloud page receives selected-project package/install/output title and detail copy instead of presenting the local readiness panel as a generic cloud account view. The stable `cloud` route now presents as Packages in navigation and localized page-title copy, keeping the user-facing label aligned with the local package workflow while preserving the existing route id. Package and local device-install metrics both read `zircon-package.toml` `source_project` metadata and compare it through `project_paths_match`, so unrelated packages or installs do not satisfy the selected project's readiness state and path spelling differences follow the same metadata-key normalization as the rest of the selected-project workflow. If the build output folder is empty, Cloud keeps the package path and package status at `Not configured` rather than resolving a relative `packages` directory from the current working directory. The visible Cloud metrics describe local mode plus reserved slots rather than account sign-in, bind through `local-mode-status` / `cloud-local-mode` while retaining older account-named fields only as additive aliases, the `HubPage::Cloud` fallback subtitle uses local package/install/service-slot wording instead of account connections, and `CloudServiceRow` no longer keeps a stale `Not connected` tone branch.

Team follows the same workspace-page row budget. Its page root inherits `PageScrollSurface` directly, the identity/repository summary uses `WorkspacePanelSection`, and the typed members panel derives from inherited `PageScrollSurface.content-height` plus a visible-row cap before handing overflow to its internal `PanelListViewport`, so selected-project Git repositories with no members or only a few recent authors do not leave a large blank list area while larger repositories remain scrollable. Its empty member explanation is passed through shared `EmptyStateBlock.detail`, so the local Git empty state still tells the user whether Hub checked the selected project first or Source Engine plus local fallback repositories without reintroducing page-local raw text or a nested panel surface. Its `HubPage::Team` fallback subtitle also stays on local Git identity/recent contributors instead of membership/collaboration settings. Cloud uses the same typed `PanelSlot` pattern for service rows, keeping lower-list headers, badges, and scroll bodies aligned with Team and catalog-style pages without reintroducing the removed generic `ListPanel`.

The 2026-05-25 Material bridge hardening records the direct-template import rule in `ui_material_usage_contract.rs` and `ui_foundation_contract.rs`. `zircon_hub/build.rs` must map `@material` to `dev/material-rust-template/material-1.0/material.slint`, `material_bridge.slint` must remain the single direct `@material` import site, and Hub wrappers must compose `MaterialWindow`, `TextField`, `OutlineButton`, `PopupMenu`, `SegmentedButton`, `ScrollView`, and Material dividers through that bridge rather than local stand-ins or page-level template imports. Toolbar search is the exception that keeps the Hub reference outlined `SearchBox`/`TextInput` density while still using the shared Material palette and tokens. `HubWindow` inherits the imported template `MaterialWindow` via `components.slint`; the Hub-specific frameless chrome and responsive shell live in `app.slint` plus the focused `shell_*_components.slint` modules. The 2026-05-28 hard cutover deleted the old `shell.slint` compatibility-note path, and the shell contracts now assert that the retired path stays absent. Team repository status also follows the selected-project context contract at the Rust projection layer. The projection compares the selected project path and discovered Git root through direct component checks, canonical paths, and `project_metadata_key` slash/case keys before labeling the row as `Selected project repository`; when a selected path exists but the discovered Git root is a fallback root, the row says the selected project repository is unavailable before showing the Source Engine repository. Only no-selection fallback roots are labeled as `Source Engine repository`, keeping the visible Source Engine name consistent across shell, Assets, Plugins, Builds, and Team. Git-root discovery itself de-duplicates candidate roots through `project_filesystem_path_key`, sharing the canonicalization path used by Assets and Plugins.
