The current asserted captures at `target/hub-visual-check/material-popupmenu-local-nav-1280x900-postbuild-0521` and `target/hub-visual-check/material-popupmenu-local-nav-1024x720-postbuild-0521` were run against the rebuilt Hub binary through the dashboard secondary-page path after `ToolbarSelect` was restored to Material `PopupMenu`; the capture script now uses a harmless focus click before business navigation, retries the Project Detail trailing-button click once inside the same hit zone when Windows frame/client-coordinate drift prevents navigation, and compares each post-click screenshot against the previous page so stale Dashboard/Browser images fail instead of being accepted under the wrong label. The 2026-05-23 Project routing captures at `target/hub-visual-check/hub-projects-continue-0523-latest-1600x1024` and `target/hub-visual-check/hub-projects-continue-0523-latest-1280x900` were produced from an isolated Hub-only locked build at `target/hub-standalone-0523\zircon_hub\Cargo.toml`, because the main workspace `cargo check/build -p zircon_hub --locked` path was blocked by an unrelated `zircon_app/Cargo.toml` workspace lockfile change. Those captures validate dashboard, new-project, project-browser, and project-detail navigation after Projects detail gained a return-subpage route, top-header interactive regions moved to stretch alignment, and the obsolete inline entry/list row properties were removed. The static contract now checks Material/Taffy coverage through bridge exports, shared wrappers, Projects, Editor, Builds, Settings, Cloud four-card metrics, PanelSlot workspace-panel shells, and data-display rows, while also asserting that the removed `ButtonStates` development surface does not return, that internal `ComponentSamples` stays out of user-facing pages, that dashboard `Show More` remains separate from `View All Projects`/list-mode Project Browser navigation, that Import Project remains a picker-backed import action instead of a plain Project Browser route, and that New Project engine choices expose both engine version and source path through the Material ListTile supporting text. The `project_management_contract` integration test keeps the secondary Projects workflow backed by executable behavior checks for metadata persistence, Editor recent isolation, enabled-template gating, recycle-bin command construction, and recent-project filtering, while the focused project workflow companion contracts keep selected-project scanner path normalization shared across Assets, Plugins, Learn, Team, Source Engine registration, source-scoped refresh routing, and project metadata. Earlier captures still cover the 1600x1024 search/filter/sort toolbar row, the 1366x820 detail layout, 1100x760 and 960x640 dashboard layouts, the Material-button-backed dashboard header, the Material text-field wrappers in Settings/Editor/Projects, the Material `PopupMenu` selector surface, the Material `SegmentedButton` wrapper in Settings, and the Material `DropDownButton` trigger plus Hub-owned engine picker.
The current responsive capture set at `target/hub-visual-check/hub-projects-responsive-1600x1024-current` and `target/hub-visual-check/hub-projects-responsive-1280x900-current` validates the Projects Dashboard, New Project, Project Browser, and Project Detail pages after ProjectFlow moved card density to container-driven `flow-width`. Dashboard project cards render as a four-card row at both checked widths, New Project keeps settings primary and templates to the right, Project Browser keeps the relaxed pinned-project list, and Project Detail keeps actions plus bound Source Engine selection visible. Project Detail now labels the Source Engine selector as `Change Source Engine`, while the current binding remains in the header subtitle, so changing engine version is presented as an explicit detail-page operation without adding another fixed-size control. The same detail action panel labels `Remove from Hub` as a non-destructive Hub-entry removal so users can distinguish it from the guarded Recycle Bin delete flow.

The 2026-05-26 Projects toolbar follow-up keeps the Dashboard grid/list segmented affordance aligned with the internal Projects view mode. The grid button remains active for `project-view-mode == "grid"`, and the list button is now active for `project-view-mode == "list"` while still routing to the Project Browser secondary page. `ui_project_navigation_contract.rs` asserts the list trigger owns both the active-state binding and the Project Browser route, so future toolbar edits cannot make the list affordance look inactive while it is the selected mode.

The same follow-up also tightens Dashboard card expansion. `ProjectFlow` now derives its column count from the actual flow width and card basis, computes the row count from `project-card-count`, keeps the collapsed state to one row, and caps expanded visible height to three rows while the inner Material `ScrollView` exposes any remaining projected cards. This preserves the reference dashboard's compact card strip, but prevents the 12-card projection limit from silently clipping cards after only two rows on wide and narrow layouts. `ui_project_layout_contract.rs` locks the column, row, visible-height, content-height, and `card-scroll-y` viewport bindings.

Project Browser now receives the same `viewport-compact` state as New Project and Project Detail. Its secondary-page `page-padding` and `page-gap` switch to compact Hub tokens when the shared responsive state reports a compact viewport, so the relaxed project list keeps proportional spacing on narrower windows instead of retaining desktop gutters. `ProjectsPage` forwards the compact state into all three Projects secondary pages, and `ui_project_layout_contract.rs` locks the Browser-local `compact-page`, compact padding, and compact gap bindings.

Project Browser result rendering now follows the same page/component split as the other Hub panels. `ProjectBrowserResultsPanel` lives in `project_browser_components.slint` and owns the result `HubPanel`, the explicit `HubVisualSpec.panel-radius` surface token, `PanelHeader`, Material-backed `PanelListViewport`, repeated `ProjectBrowserRow` delegates, and centered `EmptyStateBlock` for no matches. `ProjectBrowserPage` keeps only page/header/toolbar layout, result-panel and list-height calculations, `browser-scroll-y`, row metrics, data forwarding, and select/detail callbacks. `ui_project_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, `ui_typography_contract.rs`, and `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs` check that boundary so the Browser page does not reintroduce inline result-list internals.

Project Browser's list-first result panel now uses an explicit `ProjectBrowserTableHeader` plus a row model that shares the same `table-padding-x`, `table-gap`, thumbnail, engine, modified, status, and detail column widths with every `ProjectBrowserRow`. The selected-row background spans the same row width as the header, the trailing detail affordance owns the same detail column slot as the header spacer, and compact viewports hide engine/modified/status columns through one `compact-table` switch instead of letting header and rows collapse independently. `RecentProjectRowData.status` is projected in `projects.rs` from the same localized detail-status helper used by Project Detail, so the Browser status column can render Ready/Missing/Invalid without page-local text branching. `ui_project_browser_table_contract.rs` locks the shared column properties, page header-height reservation, panel pass-throughs, and localized row-status projection so the All Projects browser cannot regress to misaligned header labels, partial selected-row highlights, or page-owned table geometry.

The 2026-05-31 native Browser alignment capture set is `target/hub-visual-check/hub-project-browser-table-0531-v3`. It includes `hub-projects-browser.png`, `hub-projects-detail.png`, and the focused selected-row check `hub-projects-browser-selected.png`, where the Browser table header, selected row fill, status badge column, and trailing detail arrow share the same inner row width. This slice also repaired the overlay wrapper default width from the nonexistent `MaterialStyleMetrics.size_320` to `HubTokens.panel-min-sm - HubTokens.space-6`, allowing `cargo check --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --locked --offline --jobs 1` and `cargo build --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --bin zircon_hub --locked --offline --jobs 1` to pass before the real-window capture.

New Project template rail rendering follows the same page/component split. `ProjectTemplateRailPanel` lives in `project_page_components.slint` and owns the template rail's `PanelSlot` shell, `PanelHeader` count badge, shared `PanelListViewport`, repeated `TemplateChoiceRow` delegates, and selected-template callback forwarding. `TemplateChoiceRow` now uses `HubCheckBox` for the selected/unselected affordance, with a row-level `StateLayerArea` forwarding clicks to the same selected-template callback and `StatusBadge` preserving category/soon metadata. `ProjectNewPage` keeps the responsive flex mirrors, list-height calculation, `template-scroll-y`, row metrics, template data, localized title/soon label, and compact trailing-label state. `ui_project_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, `ui_input_primitives_contract.rs`, and `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs` check that boundary so the page does not reintroduce an inline template list viewport or raw checkbox/pointer handling.

New Project settings rendering follows the same split. `ProjectCreateSettingsPanel` lives in `project_page_components.slint` and owns the shared `HubContentPanelSlot` shell, header bindings, `ProjectCreateField` rows, Source Engine list, create action row, and desktop summary. `ProjectNewPage` keeps the flex sizing, height calculations, `new-engine-scroll-y`, form-ready state, localized copy, and create/browse/select callback forwarding. `ui_project_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, `ui_inputs_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, `ui_project_navigation_contract.rs`, and `ui_typography_contract.rs` check the boundary so the page does not reintroduce inline settings-panel body wiring.

The same continuation keeps the non-Projects catalog pages visually aligned. Assets now uses the same relaxed `CatalogPage` row height as Plugins and Learn (`HubTokens.list-row-lg + HubTokens.space-6`), giving long asset paths plus source/size metadata the same breathing room as plugin and learning-resource rows while preserving the shared `InfoRow` and medium-breakpoint trailing-label collapse behavior. `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs` locks the catalog-page row-height alignment.

Catalog page-specific row wrappers now live in `catalog_page_components.slint` instead of at the top of `assets.slint`, `plugins.slint`, and `learn.slint`. `AssetRow`, `PluginRow`, and `LearnRow` are exported from that component module, leaving each catalog page focused on selected-project-aware title/empty-state copy, row count, row height, and the Learn open-resource callback. `ui_data_display_contract.rs`, `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs`, and `ui_selected_project_catalog_contract.rs` check both sides of that boundary.

The 2026-05-28 Hub responsive milestone adds `ResponsiveCollapse` as the shared content-width helper for catalog and lower workspace row labels. Assets, Plugins, Learn, Cloud service rows, and Team member rows now instantiate the helper once per page and pass `label-collapse.collapsed` into their row wrappers. `ui_global_rules_contract.rs` asserts the helper remains in `layout.slint`, and `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs` locks those representative pages to the shared helper so future fixed-layout drift cannot reintroduce scattered label-collapse formulas. Validation for this slice passed focused Hub static contracts, manifest-scoped `cargo check`, full manifest-scoped `cargo test`, manifest-scoped `cargo build --bin zircon_hub`, and a 1024x768 screenshot capture at `target/hub-visual-check/hub-responsive-ui-0528-1024x768.png`.

Cloud and Team page-specific wrappers now follow the same page/component split. `cloud_page_components.slint` exports `CloudMetricSlot`, `CloudPackageActionRow`, `CloudPackageActionsPanel`, `CloudServiceRow`, and `CloudServicesPanel`, and `team_page_components.slint` exports `TeamSummarySlot`, `TeamMemberRow`, and `TeamMembersPanel`; the page roots keep responsive metric/member budgets, `WorkspacePanelSection`, list/action heights, data/copy forwarding, callbacks, and scroll bindings while the component modules own lower `PanelSlot`/`HubListPanelSlot`, `PanelHeader`, shared `ActionRow` construction, `PanelListViewport`, repeated rows, and empty-state wiring. Cloud action rows no longer import `BuildControlAction` from the Builds component module; `CloudPackageActionRow` preserves the same shared `ActionRow` behavior inside the Cloud component module, and `CloudPackageActionsPanel` now inherits `HubListPanelSlot` so package/install actions use the same title/list shell as other fixed action panels. `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, `ui_data_container_primitives_contract.rs`, and `ui_typography_contract.rs` check the combined page/component surfaces while also preventing those wrapper definitions from returning to `cloud.slint` or `team.slint`.

Team member empty state now uses `EmptyStateBlock` inside `TeamMembersPanel` instead of nesting another `EmptyStatePanel` card inside the list panel. This keeps the workspace page on one visible panel surface while still centering the no-members copy and repository path in the list viewport. `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs` and `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs` lock the typed panel plus in-panel empty-state block.

Editor's Source Engines and Build History lists now use the same in-panel `EmptyStateBlock` treatment when empty. The page keeps its existing two-list side panel, but the empty rows are token-sized, centered, and Material-text-backed instead of plain muted text floating in the list viewport. `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs` and `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs` lock this Editor empty-state alignment.

Cloud service slots now use the same in-panel empty-state treatment inside `CloudServicesPanel`. When no service rows are projected, the Services typed panel keeps its computed complete-row height and renders a centered `EmptyStateBlock` inside the list viewport instead of leaving a blank panel body. `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs` locks the Cloud service empty-state block alongside the existing metric-card and complete-row list budget checks.

The 2026-05-27 M5 workspace-gate follow-up keeps Cloud metrics on `CloudMetricSlot` as the page-local `ResponsiveSlot` wrapper while `MetricCard` remains the only metric card body. `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs` is responsible for requiring the four wrapped slots and their semantic flex sizing; `ui_typography_contract.rs` now bans only the old `component CloudMetric inherits ...` local card implementation plus the retired Team local card names, so typography extraction no longer conflicts with the layout wrapper requirement. Direct static evidence passed `ui_typography_contract.rs` at 7/7 and `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs` at 1/1 through `rustc --edition 2021 --test`; the Cargo integration entry for `ui_typography_contract` was stopped after timing out inside full `zircon_hub` Slint library compilation before any test diagnostics.

Builds Build History now follows the same in-panel empty-state rule. Its no-history block uses the larger list-empty token height, Material body padding, and centered content inside the status/history `PanelSlot`, so an empty build history no longer collapses to a single compact row. `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs` locks the Builds no-history block alongside the existing slot sizing checks.

Settings Configuration Health now follows the same in-panel empty-state rule through `SettingsConfigurationHealthPanel`. `SettingStatusRow` supports compact trailing-label collapse, while the typed panel owns the `PanelHeader`, `PanelListViewport`, status-row repetition, and centered `EmptyStateBlock` with localized `no-configuration-checks` and `configuration-health-empty-detail` copy when no checks are projected. `settings.slint` forwards only the status data/count, compact-label flag, empty-height token, and health scroll binding. `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, and `ui_typography_contract.rs` lock the Settings empty-state and compact-label behavior.

Settings build-profile and language choices now route through `SettingsBuildDefaultsPanel` in `settings_page_components.slint`. The typed panel owns the `PanelHeader`, both exported `SettingsComboChoice` rows, and the build-jobs `HubTextField`, while each combo row projects its two Hub values into Material `MenuItem` data consumed by the shared `HubComboBox`. `settings.slint` forwards only localized copy through `UiTextData` plus `build-profile`, `build-jobs`, and `language` bindings. `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs` and `ui_input_primitives_contract.rs` lock the wrapper instances and shared combo consumption so future Settings edits cannot reintroduce duplicate segment-button groups.

Settings toolchain path fields now route through `SettingsToolchainPanel` in `settings_page_components.slint`. The typed panel owns the `PanelHeader` and three exported `SettingsToolchainField` rows; each field inherits `HubTextField` and owns the shared label/placeholder/text binding shape for Python, Cargo, and Rustup executable paths. `settings.slint` forwards only localized copy through `UiTextData` plus `python-path`, `cargo-path`, and `rustup-path` bindings. `ui_inputs_contract.rs` locks the three wrapper instances and the original bindings.

Settings page-specific wrappers now live in `settings_page_components.slint` instead of at the top of `settings.slint`. `SettingsToolchainPanel`, `SettingsBuildDefaultsPanel`, `SettingsDefaultPathsPanel`, and `SettingsConfigurationHealthPanel` are typed `PanelSlot`-backed wrappers, and the same module exports their lower-level `PathSettingRow`, `SettingStatusRow`, `SettingsToolchainField`, `SettingsComboChoice`, and `SettingsSaveActionRow` helpers. This keeps `SettingsPage` focused on workspace-section layout, typed panel sizing, data/scroll forwarding, the save action call site, and existing browse/save callbacks while centralizing repeated `HubTextField`, `HubComboBox`, `PillButton`, `InfoRow`, `PanelHeader`, `PanelListViewport`, and `EmptyStateBlock` construction. The save action wrapper owns the right-aligned primary `PillButton`, icon binding, and callback forwarding while the page supplies only the responsive button width and localized label. `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_inputs_contract.rs`, `ui_input_primitives_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, and `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs` check both sides of that boundary.

Dashboard page-specific wrappers now live in `project_dashboard_components.slint` instead of at the top of `project_dashboard.slint`. `ProjectCover`, `ProjectCard`, `ProjectFlow`, `DashboardProjectCardsSection`, `DashboardToolbar`, `DashboardQuickActionRow`, `DashboardRecentProjectsPanel`, and `DashboardQuickActionsPanel` are exported from that component module, keeping Dashboard page code focused on toolbar basis/height inputs, card-section inputs, lower `PanelGrid` sizing, row-height inputs, and callback/data forwarding while centralizing the repeated toolbar `ResponsiveSlot` flow, `SearchBox`, Project filter/sort selectors, grid/list view buttons, card visuals, Material state layer, Material scroll view, empty card panel, Show More `PillButton`, recent-project `DataTable`, quick-action `PanelListViewport`, quick-action empty state, and quick-action row construction. `ui_project_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_project_navigation_contract.rs`, `ui_inputs_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, `ui_project_scope_contract.rs`, and `ui_typography_contract.rs` check both sides of that boundary.

The 2026-05-29 reference-dashboard alignment pass keeps `DashboardButtonStatesStrip` as a reference-only overlay for matching `docs/ui-and-layout/hub.png`. `HubWindow` places that overlay at `height - dashboard-bottom-strip-height`, with the strip height including the extra panel gap so the bottom Button States band occupies the same final viewport zone as the reference and no page background bleeds below it. The strip's state colors now live in `HubVisualSpec` beside the neutral reference-gray page/chrome/panel tokens, so the Dashboard sample band stays deterministic without adding private color literals to page components. Presentation-edge fixture normalization stays in the UI layer: project cards render `Modified 1d ago` as `Modified yesterday`, the recent-project table renders it as `Yesterday`, and the Sands of Time card shows `Linux` without changing persisted project metadata. Project covers now render the seeded PNGs without an extra dark overlay, the Projects filter/sort `ToolbarSelect` trigger keeps Material `OutlineButton`/`PopupMenu` behavior while drawing a neutral Hub shell, top-header status pills use reference-matched Running/standard/Error widths, Dashboard Quick Actions use the reference header/list rhythm, and Dashboard cards use the 22px reference horizontal gap. `view_model/media.rs` also maps the seeded Neon Streets fixture to the reference PNG cover so the Recent Projects thumbnail remains inspectable in screenshot comparison. The latest validation capture is `target/hub-visual-check/hub-reference-aligned-1568x1003-v65/hub-projects-dashboard.png`; pixel comparison against the provided reference improved from v56 full-frame mean/RMS `14.01`/`31.13` to v65 `10.77`/`29.53`, with toolbar selects at `10.95`/`29.96`, Quick Actions at `13.39`/`37.22`, card covers at `15.08`/`26.05`, and the bottom strip at `8.02`/`22.86`. Focused acceptance for this visual slice includes `ui_shell_header_contract.rs`, `ui_inputs_contract.rs`, `ui_project_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_data_display_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, `ui_foundation_contract.rs`, `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs`, `ui_visual_standard_contract.rs`, a manifest-scoped `zircon_hub` build, and real Windows Hub captures through `capture-hub-project-pages.ps1`.

The 2026-05-30 AI-directed web-reference closeout records its final evidence in `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-web-reference/ACCEPTANCE_EVIDENCE.md`. That ledger lists the HTML/CSS export command, the 19 generated final `hub-*.png` files, `validate-visuals.mjs`, `validate-interactions.mjs`, `validate-design-board.mjs`, and the focused `cargo test --manifest-path zircon_hub/Cargo.toml --locked --offline --jobs 1 --test ui_visual_standard_contract` gate. It also records the Export port policy, manifest schema, schema subset validation, negative schema self-tests, strict `ZIRCON_HUB_WEB_REFERENCE_PORT` behavior, interaction temporary profile cleanup with no `zircon-hub-cdp-*` temp profile left behind, AI drafts and the three design-board PNGs as review support rather than final acceptance evidence, and that an optional `cargo check` attempt timed out under concurrent Hub library compilation without replacing the focused visual contract result.

The follow-up Project Browser web-reference alignment fix keeps the design PNG's `All Projects` table on one shared column model. `styles.css` now applies `display: grid`, the same six-column template, and cell overflow rules to both `.browser-head` and `.browser-row`, so the Name, Engine, Platform, Modified, and Status header labels line up with the row cells in `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-projects-browser.png`. `validate-responsive.mjs` checks the header edge, row edge, selected-row edge, per-column left, and per-column width deltas for Browser pages, while `ui_visual_standard_contract.rs` statically locks the CSS snippets and responsive validator checks.

Builds page-specific wrappers now live in `builds_page_components.slint` instead of at the top of `builds.slint`. `BuildSourceSummaryPanel` and `BuildTaskHistoryPanel` remain Builds-owned `PanelSlot` components, while `BuildControlsPanel` and `BuildPipelinePanel` now inherit `HubListPanelSlot` so their title plus fixed action/pipeline rows consume the shared list-panel shell instead of owning a local `PanelHeader` plus repeated row stack. `BuildControlAction`, `BuildPipelineStep`, and `BuildSourceSummaryRow` stay in that component module as row-level helpers. The shared `OperationTimelinePanel` now lives in `operation_timeline_components.slint` and is re-exported by `components.slint`, so Builds and Settings consume one Material `ListTile` row and empty-state treatment for persisted action history without a Settings-to-Builds dependency. The Builds component module owns the Source Build header, source/output summary rows, profile/jobs badges, selected-project Build/Open Output/Open Editor/Package/Install rows, pipeline steps, current-task status, build-history viewport, and build-history empty state. `BuildsPage` now keeps only `WorkspacePanelSection` composition, semantic `basis`/`grow`/`order` and paired Slint flex properties, selected-project/source-engine/status data forwarding, build-history and operation-timeline scroll bindings, compact label collapse, and callback forwarding. `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, `ui_data_container_primitives_contract.rs`, `ui_selected_project_runtime_contract.rs`, `ui_data_display_contract.rs`, and `ui_typography_contract.rs` check both sides of that boundary: Builds component definitions and row internals stay in `builds_page_components.slint`, shared timeline internals stay in `operation_timeline_components.slint`, and `builds.slint` keeps one call each to the five typed panels plus original source path, output path, last-build, profile, status, job-count, selected-project, action callback, build-history, and operation-timeline bindings.

Editor page-specific wrappers now live in `editor_page_components.slint` instead of at the top of `editor.slint`. `SourceEngineRow`, `EditorSideListPanel`, `EditorSourceEngineListPanel`, `EditorBuildHistoryPanel`, `EditorPathFieldRow`, `EditorActionRow`, `EditorActionsPanel`, `EditorSourceSummaryPanel`, `EditorSourceSettingsPanel`, and `EditorSourceSummaryRow` are exported from that component module, keeping the Editor page responsible for panel layout, typed panel call sites, scroll bindings, data forwarding, and Source Engine selection/removal callbacks. The component module owns the repeated Material `ListTile`, `HubPanel` side-list shell, typed Source Engine and Build History list bodies, shared `PanelListViewport` rows, empty-state blocks, `HubTextField` plus browse/rename button rows, the `HubListPanelSlot`-backed Editor Actions panel, the `HubContentPanelSlot`-backed source summary panel, the `HubFormPanelSlot`-backed source settings panel, `ActionRow`, and `InfoRow` construction.

The Editor contracts check both sides of that boundary. `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_inputs_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, `ui_typography_contract.rs`, and `ui_data_display_contract.rs` require the exported component definitions in `editor_page_components.slint`; `editor.slint` must keep one typed source summary panel call, one typed actions panel call, one typed source settings panel call, one typed Source Engine list panel call, one typed Build History panel call, original source path, output path, active engine, status, job-count, callback bindings, and the `actions-first` compact ordering rule that places the actions panel above the overview when the first viewport is too short for both. It must not reintroduce inline `EditorActionRow`, `EditorSourceSummaryRow`, `EditorPathFieldRow`, `SourceEngineRow`, or `BuildHistoryRow` loops.

The 2026-05-26 Hub layout continuation reran package-level validation with the warmed target directory at `D:\cargo-targets\zircon-hub-projects-0526-final`: `cargo fmt -p zircon_hub --check`, `cargo check --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --locked --offline --jobs 1`, `cargo test --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --locked --offline --jobs 1`, and `cargo build --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --bin zircon_hub --locked --offline --jobs 1` all passed. The same slice captured Projects Dashboard, New Project, Project Browser, and Project Detail at `target\hub-visual-check\hub-layout-continue-0526-1280x900`, `target\hub-visual-check\hub-layout-continue-0526-1024x720-detail-fixed`, and `target\hub-visual-check\hub-layout-continue-0526-1024x720-default-fixed`; the final compact run verifies the multi-page screenshot script's Project Detail default click after it moved to the first-row trailing affordance. Focused workspace captures for Packages/Cloud, Team, and Editor were written beside each Projects set and visually checked for obvious overlap, black bands, bottom status-bar obstruction, and stale-page navigation.

The 2026-05-24 delete-confirm capture set at `target/hub-visual-check/hub-projects-1600x1024-delete-confirm` validates Dashboard, New Project, Project Browser, Project Detail, and the Project Detail pending-delete state after the guarded warning moved to `StatusBanner`. The final focused capture sets at `target/hub-visual-check/hub-projects-1024x720-statusbanner-final-0524` and `target/hub-visual-check/hub-projects-1600x1024-statusbanner-final-0524` validate the same flow after the pending confirmation moved to the top of the Actions panel, the normal action/change-engine controls hide while pending, and `StatusBanner` stopped stretching into a tall empty block on wide Detail layouts. The multi-page screenshot skill now exposes `-CapturePendingDelete`, `-DeleteClickX/-DeleteClickY`, and `-DeleteScrollNotches`, records those click defaults in `hub-project-pages-clicks.json`, captures `hub-projects-detail-delete-ready.png` after compact Detail scrolling, and fails the run if `hub-projects-detail-delete-confirm.png` does not differ enough from that ready baseline after clicking Delete Project.

The 2026-05-24 toolbar-chevron captures at `target/hub-visual-check/hub-projects-toolbar-chevron-1280x900-0524` and `target/hub-visual-check/hub-projects-toolbar-chevron-1600x1024-0524` validate Project Browser plus the open filter and sort `PopupMenu` states after `ToolbarSelect` gained the independent right-aligned Material chevron icon. The captured toolbars keep the chevrons on the far right of the select triggers, vertically centered against the button height, while menu anchors remain under the correct trigger. The 1600 capture also keeps the wide top status pills visible while Dashboard, New Project, Browser, and Detail stay inside the Projects secondary-page structure.

The 2026-05-25 Settings bridge captures reused the existing rebuilt Hub binary at `D:\cargo-targets\zircon-hub-settings-0525\debug\zircon_hub.exe` and wrote `target/hub-visual-check/settings-bridge-1600/hub-settings.png`, `target/hub-visual-check/settings-bridge-1366/hub-settings.png`, `target/hub-visual-check/settings-bridge-1100/hub-settings.png`, and `target/hub-visual-check/settings-bridge-960/hub-settings-header-click2.png`. These captures cover the planned 1600x1024, 1366x820, 1100x760, and 960x640 Settings breakpoints after the direct Material template bridge contract was hardened; text fields, the Settings build-default combo rows, path rows, and configuration-health rows remain inside shared page/panel/list surfaces without visible text collision or control overflow. The follow-up MaterialWindow capture at `target/hub-visual-check/material-window-bridge-1280/hub-dashboard.png` verifies the imported template window wrapper still renders the Hub frameless shell, background, sidebar, dashboard, and bottom status bar without black bands or chrome overlap.

The `hub-ui-polish-foundations-inputs-navigation-contracts-docs` lock-down milestone adds a focused API-level contract over the input/navigation primitive family. `components.slint` explicitly re-exports the stable input primitives (`SelectOptionData`, `SearchBox`, `HubTextField`, `ToolbarSelect`, `DropDownButton`, and `SegmentButton`), the shared navigation DTO `NavItemData`, `NavRail`, and the Material adapter DTOs (`NavigationItem`, `MenuItem`, and Material style objects) that callers need to build or adapt toolbar and rail data without importing lower-level owner files directly. `ui_input_navigation_api_contract.rs` is intentionally narrower than the existing visual-state tests: it checks public property, callback, DTO, and adapter-routing shapes, while `ui_inputs_contract.rs` and `ui_navigation_contract.rs` continue to check internal state-layer, Material-control, and shell forwarding details. Downstream shell and page polish should therefore consume the wrappers through `components.slint` and route collapsed navigation through the Rust `NavItemData` to `NavigationItem` adapter rather than constructing a second page-routing model.

The 2026-05-31 bottom-up Material component pass fills the remaining low-level control gap before more page assembly. `inputs.slint` now owns `HubCheckBox`, `HubCheckBoxRow`, `HubSwitch`, `HubToggleRow`, and `HubComboBox` over the local Material template's `CheckBox`, `CheckBoxTile`, `Switch`, and shared `HubDropDownSurface`; `navigation.slint` owns `HubTabs` over `TabBar` and `SecondaryTabBar`. These wrappers are re-exported through `components.slint` so higher-level lists, settings panels, drawers, and window surfaces can compose checkbox/toggle/tabs/combo behavior without page-local `TouchArea` or text-input clones. `HubToggleRow` keeps its text column left aligned and its `HubSwitch` in the trailing slot through Slint stretch layout rather than root-width arithmetic, avoiding layout recursion while preserving the expected row shape. Settings build defaults now exercise that path through `SettingsComboChoice`, which maps two stored values to `MenuItem` rows, initializes the Material `current-index` from the stored value, and binds later selections back to `build-profile` and `language`. New Project template choices now exercise the checkbox path through `TemplateChoiceRow`, where `HubCheckBox` mirrors `ProjectTemplateData.selected` and the row-level Material state layer forwards enabled template clicks. Project Detail now exercises the toggle path through `ProjectDetailPinToggleRow`, which inherits `HubToggleRow`, mirrors `ProjectDetailData.pinned`, and forwards the existing `toggle-pin` callback while leaving Open, Remove, and Delete as command buttons. `ui_inputs_contract.rs`, `ui_input_primitives_contract.rs`, `ui_project_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, and `ui_navigation_contract.rs` lock the new wrapper layer and reject hand-rolled interaction internals.

The same bottom-up pass now promotes the next container layer. `overlays.slint` owns `HubPopupMenu`, `HubDropDownSurface`, `HubDrawer`, `HubModalDrawer`, `HubWindowView`, `PopupPanel`, and `HubPopupWindow` as wrappers over the local Material `PopupMenu`, `DropDownMenu`, `Drawer`, `ModalDrawer`, `MaterialWindow`, `PopupWindow`, and the Hub popup panel chrome; `HubPopupWindow` centralizes close-on-click-outside behavior plus popup `x`/`y`/width/height forwarding, while `app.slint` inherits `HubWindowView` so window sizing and frameless chrome constraints no longer live in the page shell. `ToolbarSelect` now consumes `HubPopupMenu` for Project Browser filter/sort menus, keeping the Material popup behavior behind the shared overlay wrapper while leaving the toolbar trigger geometry in `inputs.slint`; `HubComboBox` now consumes `HubDropDownSurface` for Settings build-profile and language choices, keeping the public `items`/`current-index`/`selected` adapter API while removing the direct `DropDownMenu` import from `inputs.slint`; top-header Source Engine and user popups now consume `HubPopupWindow` plus `PopupPanel` instead of owning raw `PopupWindow`/`HubPanel` directly, so close policy and anchor geometry stay in `overlays.slint` while the body chrome stays in `PopupPanel`. `data_display.slint` owns generic `HubListView`, `HubListPanelSlot`, and `PanelListViewport`, `table_view_components.slint` owns `HubTableView`, `HubTableBody`, `TableColumnHeader`, `ProjectTableRow`, and `DataTable`, and `tree_view_components.slint` owns `TreeItemData`, `HubTreeRow`, and `HubTreeView` on top of the shared row/viewport primitives. This gives later pages separate Material-style list/table/tree layers before page-specific rows are assembled without letting one file collect every container type. `CatalogListPanel` now inherits `HubListView` instead of hand-building a second scroll shell, while preserving the catalog header, badge tone, vertical padding, empty-state height, and centered empty copy used by Assets, Plugins, and Learn. `HubListPanelSlot` fills the same role for responsive `PanelSlot` children: Cloud Services, Team Members, Settings Default Paths, Settings Configuration Health, the New Project template rail, and Dashboard Quick Actions inherit it, keep their existing page-level height and scroll bindings, and no longer duplicate `PanelHeader` plus `PanelListViewport` shells in their page component modules. The wrapper exposes a `header-height` override so dense reference panels such as Quick Actions can keep the shorter Dashboard header without forking the shared shell. Editor's Source Engines and Build History side-list panels now inherit `HubListView` through `EditorSideListPanel`, keeping their existing title/badge and scroll bindings while removing another page-local `HubPanel`/`PanelListViewport` shell. `ProjectBrowserResultsPanel` now inherits `HubTableView` and places `ProjectBrowserTableHeader` plus `HubTableBody` inside that shared table shell, so the Browser keeps its stable header-above-rows geometry while consuming the generic table viewport for scroll, row spacing, and empty-state behavior. Dashboard Recent Projects now also reaches the same table body layer: `DataTable` keeps its transparent in-panel table header/divider and `ProjectTableRow` column model, but its scrollable row body is `HubTableBody` instead of a page-local `ScrollView`/content-height implementation. `ui_overlay_primitives_contract.rs`, `ui_data_display_contract.rs`, `ui_data_container_primitives_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs`, `ui_typography_contract.rs`, `ui_project_browser_table_contract.rs`, `ui_shell_composition_contract.rs`, `ui_shell_header_contract.rs`, `ui_visual_standard_contract.rs`, and `ui_shell_window_contract.rs` lock these boundaries. The Cloud/Team panel-slot slice was captured through the native Windows Hub window at `target/hub-visual-check/cloud-team-hublistpanelslot-0531/hub-cloud.png` and `target/hub-visual-check/cloud-team-hublistpanelslot-0531/hub-team.png`; the Settings slice was captured at `target/hub-visual-check/settings-hublistpanelslot-0531/hub-settings.png`; the New Project template rail slice was captured at `target/hub-visual-check/template-rail-hublistpanelslot-0531/hub-new-project.png`; the Quick Actions slice was captured at `target/hub-visual-check/dashboard-quickactions-hublistpanelslot-0531/hub-projects-dashboard.png` and with seeded reference projects at `target/hub-visual-check/dashboard-quickactions-hublistpanelslot-0531/seeded/hub-projects-dashboard.png`; the toolbar-select overlay slice was captured at `target/hub-visual-check/toolbarselect-hubpopupmenu-0531/hub-projects-browser-filter-menu.png` and `target/hub-visual-check/toolbarselect-hubpopupmenu-0531/hub-projects-browser-sort-menu.png`; the combo overlay slice was captured at `target/hub-visual-check/settings-combo-hubdropdownsurface-0531/hub-settings.png` and `target/hub-visual-check/settings-combo-hubdropdownsurface-0531/hub-settings-build-profile-menu.png`; the top-header popup panel slice was captured at `target/hub-visual-check/top-header-popuppanel-0531/hub-source-engine-popup.png` and `target/hub-visual-check/top-header-popuppanel-0531/hub-user-menu.png`; the top-header popup-window shell slice was captured at `target/hub-visual-check/top-header-hubpopupwindow-0531/hub-source-engine-popup.png` and `target/hub-visual-check/top-header-hubpopupwindow-0531/hub-user-menu.png`; the top-header user-menu `ActionRow` slice was captured at `target/hub-visual-check/top-header-actionrow-0531/hub-user-menu.png`; the top-header Source Engine `InfoRow` slice was captured at `target/hub-visual-check/top-header-engine-inforow-0531/hub-source-engine-popup-final.png`. These captures validate that the shared panel, row, and overlay shells keep page structure, popup/menu anchors, badges, row rhythm, selector/action-row affordances, and fixed bottom status chrome stable at `1568x1003`.
The Builds list-panel continuation extends that `HubListPanelSlot` usage to `BuildControlsPanel` and `BuildPipelinePanel`: both panels keep their page-level height and callback/data bindings, but their title, row count, row spacing, and repeated `BuildControlAction`/`BuildPipelineStep` delegates now live inside the shared list-panel shell instead of local `PanelHeader` plus hand-stacked rows. The same action-panel slice moves `EditorActionsPanel` and `CloudPackageActionsPanel` to `HubListPanelSlot`, so Editor actions, Cloud package/install actions, Builds controls, and Builds pipeline all share the same fixed-row list-panel wrapper. The native Builds capture at `target/hub-visual-check/builds-hublistpanelslot-0531/hub-builds.png` validates that the Selected Project Actions and Build Pipeline panels keep their row rhythm, badge alignment, disabled action treatment, and bottom status-bar clearance at `1568x1003`; the follow-up Editor/Cloud captures at `target/hub-visual-check/editor-cloud-actions-hublistpanelslot-0531/hub-editor.png` and `target/hub-visual-check/editor-cloud-actions-hublistpanelslot-0531/hub-cloud.png` validate the same shared action-panel shell on Editor Actions and Cloud Packages.

The Dashboard table-shell continuation moves `DashboardRecentProjectsPanel` from a local `PanelSlot` header/body stack to `HubTableView`. `HubTableView` now exposes the same semantic `basis`/`grow`/`shrink`/`order` inputs used by responsive panel call sites plus a `header-height` override, so the Dashboard Recent Projects panel keeps the reference compact header while sharing the same Material table shell as Project Browser. `DataTable` still owns the transparent in-panel table header, divider, `ProjectTableRow` column model, and `HubTableBody` scroll body; the page root only supplies lower-grid flex geometry, data, labels, and callbacks. `ui_data_container_primitives_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, `ui_project_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, and `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs` lock the new table-panel boundary. The native Dashboard capture at `target/hub-visual-check/dashboard-recent-hubtableview-0531/hub-projects-dashboard-single.png` validates the seeded Recent Projects table, compact header, footer action, Quick Actions neighbor panel, and bottom status chrome at `1568x1003`.

The Dashboard Recent Projects action-button continuation keeps that footer action on the same command-button primitive used by shell and form actions. `HubCommandButton` now exposes an optional trailing icon lane, so compact link-style actions such as View All Projects can reserve the right-side arrow without rebuilding button chrome. `DashboardRecentProjectsPanel` consumes `HubCommandButton` directly with the Projects leading icon, inherited Material `StateLayerArea`, shared label typography, and trailing chevron, while preserving the existing `view-all()` callback. The panel no longer declares a local `Rectangle`, `StateLayerArea`, `HorizontalLayout`, `Image`, or `MaterialText` button body for this action; `ui_inputs_contract.rs`, `ui_data_container_primitives_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, and `ui_project_layout_contract.rs` lock the shared command-button path.

The Dashboard toolbar view-toggle continuation moves the grid/list pair into the same button-family module. `HubViewToggleButton` inherits `HubIconButton` and owns the compact active/idle fill, stroke, foreground, state-layer, radius, and icon-size tokens used by Dashboard view switching. `HubViewToggleGroup` owns the two grid/list icons, selected-mode state projection, group spacing, and `selected(string)` callback. `DashboardToolbar` now supplies only toolbar sizing, `project-view-mode`, and the page/subpage forwarding logic, instead of retaining a page-local `DashboardViewToggleButton` definition or two local toggle-button wrappers. `ui_inputs_contract.rs`, `ui_project_layout_contract.rs`, and `ui_project_navigation_contract.rs` lock the shared toggle-group boundary. Verification passed focused direct static contracts for inputs, project layout, project navigation, Material usage, visual standard, panel slot, and data-container primitives, plus manifest-scoped `cargo check --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --locked --offline --jobs 1`.

The Project source-engine list continuation moves `ProjectEngineChoiceList` itself onto the shared list viewport layer: it now directly inherits `PanelListViewport` instead of owning a transparent `Rectangle` plus local `engine-list` child. New Project and Project Detail still pass their own `row-height`, `row-spacing`, `empty-height`, selected/registered labels, scroll binding, and compact-label inputs into that shared inherited surface, while `EngineChoiceRow` remains the row-level Material `ListTile` delegate. `ui_data_container_primitives_contract.rs`, `ui_project_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, `ui_inputs_contract.rs`, `ui_typography_contract.rs`, and `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs` lock the direct viewport boundary and the call-site bindings. The native Projects capture at `target/hub-visual-check/project-engine-choice-list-direct-0531` validates Dashboard, New Project, Browser, and Detail after the wrapper removal; the accepted New Project and Detail screenshots are `hub-projects-new-project.png` and `hub-projects-detail.png`.

The Project Detail section continuation adds `HubSection` in `surfaces.slint` as a lightweight in-panel section primitive: it centralizes `PanelHeader` plus arbitrary child content without `HubPanel` or `PanelSlot` chrome, so it can be used inside an existing card without creating nested cards. `ProjectDetailInfoSection` and `ProjectDetailEngineSection` now inherit `HubSection`; the page still supplies the section heights, spacing, project data, row metrics, engine list metrics, and callbacks, while the component module owns only the section title/subtitle and row/list delegates. `ui_foundation_contract.rs`, `ui_project_layout_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, `ui_typography_contract.rs`, and `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs` lock the new primitive and both Project Detail consumers. The native Project Detail capture at `target/hub-visual-check/project-detail-hubsection-0531/hub-projects-detail.png` validates that the Actions and Project Information panels remain single-card structures with stable section spacing, source-engine rows, and bottom status chrome at `1568x1003`.

Builds now consumes the same in-panel section primitive for its current task and build-history stack. `BuildTaskHistoryPanel` remains the owning `PanelSlot`, but its Current Task and Build History subsections are `HubSection` blocks instead of duplicated local `PanelHeader` stacks. The component intentionally keeps explicit `current-task-section-height`, `history-list-height`, and `history-section-height` calculations rather than stretching both sections equally; the stretched prototype cropped the no-history empty state near the fixed bottom status bar. `ui_data_container_primitives_contract.rs`, `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs`, and `ui_typography_contract.rs` lock the section-height model, the `PanelListViewport` history body, and the MaterialText status title. The 2026-05-31 verification reran `cargo fmt --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --check`, the focused Hub UI contract set (`ui_data_container_primitives_contract`, `ui_foundation_contract`, `ui_workspace_layout_contract`, `ui_panel_slot_contract`, `ui_typography_contract`, and `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract`), `cargo build --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --bin zircon_hub --locked --offline --jobs 1`, and the native screenshot capture `target/hub-visual-check/buildtaskhistory-hubsection-0531/hub-builds-final.png` at `1568x1003`. The final capture shows the Current Task and Build History sections in one right-hand panel with the no-history empty text visible above the bottom status bar.

The same 2026-05-31 design-reference pass regenerated `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-projects-browser.png` with `node docs\ui-and-layout\hub-web-reference\export-pages.mjs --only=hub-projects-browser` and reran `node docs\ui-and-layout\hub-web-reference\validate-responsive.mjs` plus `node docs\ui-and-layout\hub-web-reference\validate-visuals.mjs`. The responsive validator checks the All Projects table, header, every browser row, selected-row fill, left/right edges, and column widths across the `1915x508` design-review crop plus the standard responsive viewport set, preventing the previously reported Browser table header/row misalignment from returning in the HTML/CSS reference artifacts. The visual validator keeps the exported PNG inventory, export index, spot-check ledger, docs matrix, AI manifest coverage, and dashboard pixel comparison synchronized with the regenerated Browser reference.

The operation timeline continuation extracts the shared timeline row and panel from the oversized `data_display.slint` into `operation_timeline_components.slint`. `data_display.slint` now stays focused on generic list primitives plus catalog/data rows, while `OperationTimelineRow` owns the action-history-specific `OperationTimelineRowData` binding and status icon. `OperationTimelinePanel` now inherits `HubListPanelSlot`, so its title, badge, `PanelListViewport`, scroll binding, row-height forwarding, compact empty-height, and empty-state body are described by the shared list-panel primitive instead of a local `PanelSlot` plus duplicated header/list shell. `components.slint` remains the public import surface, so Builds, Cloud, and Settings still consume `OperationTimelinePanel` through the same shared component entry point. `ui_data_display_contract.rs`, `ui_foundation_contract.rs`, `ui_panel_slot_contract.rs`, `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs`, and `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs` lock the module boundary, including the rule that `data_display.slint` must not regain operation-timeline ownership and the rule that the timeline panel must not reintroduce local `PanelHeader`/`PanelListViewport` chrome. The 2026-05-31 verification reran `cargo fmt --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --check`, focused Hub UI contracts (`ui_data_display_contract`, `ui_foundation_contract`, `ui_panel_slot_contract`, `ui_workspace_layout_contract`, and `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract`), `cargo build --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --bin zircon_hub --locked --offline --jobs 1`, and native captures at `target/hub-visual-check/operation-timeline-module-0531/hub-builds-1568x1003.png` plus the supplemental tall timeline check `target/hub-visual-check/operation-timeline-module-0531/hub-builds-operation-timeline-1568x1200.png`.

The table-view continuation extracts `TableColumnHeader`, `ProjectTableRow`, `HubTableBody`, `DataTable`, and `HubTableView` into `table_view_components.slint`. The module remains below the public `components.slint` entrypoint, imports only the shared `PanelListViewport` from `data_display.slint`, and keeps the Dashboard Recent Projects and Project Browser table APIs unchanged. `data_display.slint` is now 539 lines and cannot regain table component ownership under `ui_data_display_contract.rs` and `ui_data_container_primitives_contract.rs`; `ui_foundation_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs`, and `ui_visual_standard_contract.rs` also include the new module in the focused component and visual-token checks. The 2026-05-31 focused static verification ran `cargo test --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --locked --offline --jobs 1 --test ui_data_display_contract --test ui_data_container_primitives_contract --test ui_foundation_contract --test ui_material_usage_contract --test ui_page_surface_coverage_contract --test ui_visual_standard_contract --message-format short --color never`, covering the table module split before the final build and native screenshot check.

The tree-view continuation extracts `TreeItemData`, `HubTreeRow`, and `HubTreeView` into `tree_view_components.slint`. The new module remains below the public `components.slint` entrypoint, imports `HubRowSurface` and `PanelListViewport` from `data_display.slint`, and keeps the Material `ListTile`, disclosure `StateLayerArea`, neutral avatar tokens, and trailing `StatusBadge` behavior together in the tree family instead of growing the list/data-row module. `data_display.slint` is back under 720 lines and cannot regain tree component ownership under `ui_data_container_primitives_contract.rs`; `ui_foundation_contract.rs`, `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs`, and `ui_visual_standard_contract.rs` include the new module in the component-export, real-page-surface, and visual-token checks. This slice is structural and does not change routed page visuals directly; a manifest-scoped Slint check is the authoritative validation for the new module wiring.

The 2026-05-31 Dashboard pixel-reference continuation narrows the Projects landing page toward `docs/ui-and-layout/hub.png` without claiming pixel parity. `SearchBox` now exposes a `prominent` state so the Dashboard search field can hold the cyan reference outline even before keyboard focus. Dashboard toolbar select controls use the reduced `project-dashboard-toolbar-select-ratio` token, center their compact button height, and route the grid/list affordance through the shared `HubViewToggleGroup` shape so the toolbar tracks the reference icon-button proportions without owning the button pair locally. `ProjectCover` owns absolute cover overlays for the card menu and Zircon mark plate, and `HubFlowNextButton` now owns the collapsed-card carousel affordance at the right edge of the card row. Dashboard Quick Actions now use a Dashboard-specific compact `DashboardQuickActionRow` backed by Material `StateLayerArea` and the existing quick-action ids/callbacks, while preserving the reference copy for Build Project, Install to Device, Package Project, and Open in Editor. The top header notification, help, and settings actions use a transparent `HeaderPlainIconButton` instead of framed Hub icon buttons so the shell chrome is closer to the supplied image. The verification capture set is `target/hub-visual-check/pixel-match-0531-j`; its Dashboard screenshot is `hub-projects-dashboard.png` at `1568x1003`. The reference comparison against `docs/ui-and-layout/hub.png` currently measures full-frame mean `10.06` and RMS `28.83`; the largest remaining regional gaps are project cards mean `14.07`, top bar mean `12.09`, sidebar mean `11.92`, and quick panel mean `9.20`. This is an improvement over the prior full-frame mean `10.48`/RMS `29.81`, but exact pixel-level parity still requires another pass on card imagery, shell/sidebar tone, and font/rendering offsets. The same slice passed `cargo build --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --bin zircon_hub --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never`, the focused contract set covering dashboard layout, panel slots, foundations, data display, Material usage, page-surface coverage, and visual-standard checks, plus `cargo fmt --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --check`.

The follow-up Dashboard pixel-reference pass keeps the target as `docs/ui-and-layout/hub.png` and validates only through native Windows Hub captures. The capture script now moves the cursor out of the Dashboard before the first save and uses a wide-layout New Project click ratio that works without manual override. Layout/color convergence tightened the sidebar rail inner width, top-header brand spacing, Projects header vertical offset, New Project split-button geometry, header action icon vertical centering, non-running Engine Status success color, and chrome background tone. The accepted capture set is `target/hub-visual-check/pixel-match-0531-ad`; `hub-projects-dashboard.png` is `1568x1003` and compares against the reference at full-frame mean `9.30` / RMS `27.87`. Regional means are topbar `10.66`, sidebar `9.54`, workspace `9.37`, header actions `13.97`, project cards `12.75`, recent panel `9.79`, quick panel `9.12`, and bottom strip `8.01`. Exact pixel-level parity is still not met; the remaining highest gaps are the project cover/card raster content, header action font/icon antialiasing, and text-rendering offsets inside the table/action panels.

The 2026-06-01 bottom-up icon-button continuation adds `HubIconButton` as the shared Hub-level icon button primitive beside the Material-template-backed `IconButton`. `IconButton` remains the generic Filled/Outlined Material wrapper, while `HubIconButton` centralizes the reference-tuned square/circle chrome used by transparent titlebar icons, Dashboard grid/list toggles, and the project-card flow next affordance: tokenized width/height/radius, active/idle backgrounds, borders, foreground colors, disabled opacity, focus ring, and `StateLayerArea` interaction are now one primitive instead of repeated page-local `Rectangle` plus `Image` blocks. The icon-button module split later moved `IconButton`, `HubIconButton`, `HubTopbarIconButton`, `HubBackButton`, `HubFlowNextButton`, `HubRowActionButton`, `HubViewToggleButton`, `HubViewToggleGroup`, `HubFloatingIconButton`, and `HubMoreMenuButton` into `icon_button_components.slint`; direct consumers import typed icon wrappers from that owner while `button_components.slint` keeps text, command, sidebar, user-menu, panel, and window button wrappers. Focused static contracts now lock the primitive API and prevent the header from reintroducing `HeaderPlainIconButton`. The native capture set `target/hub-visual-check/hubiconbutton-0601` preserved the previous Dashboard comparison exactly at full-frame mean `9.30` / RMS `27.87`, so this slice improves component layering without regressing the current pixel-alignment baseline.

The same 2026-06-01 command-button continuation adds `HubCommandButton` as the shared text-plus-icon command primitive for reference Projects header actions. It preserves the existing Import Project and New Project geometry while moving the local `HeaderActionButton` chrome out of `shell_page_components.slint`: tokenized width/height, focused border, primary/secondary fills, Material `StateLayerArea`, centered leading icon, Material label, and optional split-menu divider/chevron are now centralized in `shared.slint`. `HubPageHeader` consumes `HubCommandButton` for both Projects header actions, and the shell composition contract rejects reintroducing a local header action button. The native capture set `target/hub-visual-check/hubcommandbutton-0601` preserved the Dashboard comparison at full-frame mean `9.30` / RMS `27.87`, with header actions still at mean `13.97`, confirming this structural extraction did not move the current pixel baseline.

The updated Hub reference workflow keeps the HTML/CSS web reference as the first-stage component and pixel target, then ports verified component decisions into the Slint/Rust Hub. The current Slint dashboard uses the promoted `HubFloatingIconButton` for project-cover overlay menus, keeps project cover rendering on the reference PNG's native `270x112` geometry through `HubVisualSpec.card-cover-height`, and aligns the Projects page header subtitle with an explicit Material `body_medium` text style instead of the generic `MutedText` body-small helper. The shell composition contract locks that page-header typography and offset so future edits do not silently drift from the reference heading stack. The native capture set `target/hub-visual-check/page-header-card-cover-0601` compares against `docs/ui-and-layout/hub.png` at full-frame mean `8.56` / RMS `27.48`; the project cards region improves to mean `8.78` and the project-cover strip to mean `4.96`, while the next largest visible gaps remain header action geometry/text antialiasing, topbar/sidebar glyph rendering, and table/action-panel text metrics.

The interactive card-surface continuation starts the Card/CardActionArea family instead of leaving Dashboard project cards as a one-off clickable panel. `HubInteractiveCardSurface` now lives in `surfaces.slint`, inherits the shared `HubPanel` Material card chrome, owns selected-vs-interactive variant selection, `interaction-foreground`, and one Material `StateLayerArea`, and exposes `@children` for typed card content. `ProjectCard` now inherits this shared card-action surface, keeps its cover, title/path/modified text, pinned/missing/version/platform badges, fixed card sizing, and cover menu button, while routing whole-card project selection through inherited `clicked` instead of declaring a local `card-state := StateLayerArea`. `ui_foundation_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, and `ui_visual_standard_contract.rs` lock the primitive, export, and ProjectCard migration while rejecting local card hit testing from returning. Focused verification passed direct static execution of `ui_foundation_contract.rs` (9/9), `ui_material_usage_contract.rs` (9/9), `ui_visual_standard_contract.rs` (8/8), `ui_project_layout_contract.rs` (1/1), and `ui_inputs_contract.rs` (7/7), Rust formatting, `git diff --check` for the touched slice, and manifest-scoped `cargo check --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --locked --offline --jobs 1 --target-dir D:\cargo-targets\zircon-hub-contract-repair-0604 --message-format short --color never`.

The HTML/CSS reference layer now has its own bottom-up Material-style component module before any further Slint porting. `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-web-reference/material-components.js` owns the web reference atoms and collections for buttons, icon buttons, search inputs, select buttons, tags, progress/check/stat rows, page headings, toolbars, project cards, project tables, quick-action lists, popup menus, confirmation modals, Source Engine and user popups, plus topbar/sidebar/workspace surface markers. `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-web-reference/material-styles.css` owns the matching reusable component density and styling for controls, cards, tables, lists, menus, popups, and modal surfaces, while `styles.css` keeps tokens plus shell/page placement. `app.js` composes those shared components instead of defining low-level control markup inline, reducing it from 999 to 815 lines in this slice. `validate-responsive.mjs` now checks that the browser shell loads both component files, that every fixed viewport/live-resize page contains the expected `data-component` coverage for its active low-level controls, list/table surfaces, popup/modal states, and window/drawer/workspace surfaces, and that `material-styles.css` retains the bottom-up style layer. The refreshed web-reference export/validation improves the dashboard comparison to full-frame mean `9.21` / RMS `27.50` by preserving the topbar Running status dot, lifting the Button States strip, and desaturating the primary Projects header action toward `docs/ui-and-layout/hub.png`; exact pixel-level parity against the fixed source image still remains a later tuning target, not a completed claim.

The 2026-06-01 Slint Dashboard button-state pass keeps that bottom-up visual contract in `HubVisualSpec` rather than page-local color literals. The Button States strip now uses darker secondary state fills and separates `button-state-icon-primary-background` from `button-state-icon-primary-hover-background`, matching the reference where the first primary icon button, the hovered primary icon button, and the non-primary active icon button are visibly distinct. `HubButtonStateIconSample` routes the active primary branch through that dedicated token, so future Dashboard state examples can tune reference colors without adding another local icon-button variant. `ui_visual_standard_contract.rs` locks the new token and the component branch. The real Hub capture set is `target/hub-visual-check/bottom-button-states-0601`; its Dashboard screenshot compares against `docs/ui-and-layout/hub.png` at full-frame mean/RMS `7.579/25.311`, improving from `7.767/25.427`, with the bottom strip improving from `7.361/22.202` to `5.982/21.211` and the Icon subsection from `15.420/30.547` to `7.484/23.550`.

The Dashboard Button States component continuation moves the remaining text and icon sample primitives into the shared button family instead of keeping them as page-local helpers. `HubButtonStateTextSample` now owns the primary, secondary, and tertiary sample widths, fills, disabled opacity, and Material label colors in `button_components.slint`; `HubButtonStateIconSample` inherits `HubIconButton`, owns the plus-icon sample chrome, preserves primary/active/disabled token routing, and keeps icon interaction inside the base button primitive. `DashboardButtonStatesStrip` remains in `project_dashboard_components.slint` because it is a Dashboard reference overlay panel, but it now only composes the shared button-state samples and strip labels. `ui_inputs_contract.rs`, `ui_project_layout_contract.rs`, and `ui_visual_standard_contract.rs` lock the button-family exports, the removal of `DashboardStateButton`/`DashboardTertiaryState`/`DashboardStateIcon`, and the tokenized button-state visual branches.

The sidebar status-card continuation keeps `NavStatusPanel` as the owner of the Engine Status card chrome and changes only its internal rhythm. The panel now uses explicit asymmetric padding (`space-6` top and `space-4 + size_2` bottom) instead of the earlier uniform `space-3`, aligning the status dot/title stack and the Check for Updates button closer to the supplied `hub.png` without moving the nav rail or collapse row. `ui_shell_composition_contract.rs` locks those tokenized padding edges so later sidebar edits do not return to anonymous uniform padding. The accepted capture set is `target/hub-visual-check/nav-status-spacing-bottom18-0601`; against `docs/ui-and-layout/hub.png`, full-frame mean/RMS improves from `7.579/25.311` to `7.553/25.245`, the sidebar from `8.180/26.403` to `7.947/25.839`, and the status card from `15.241/34.110` to `13.991/31.715`.

The button-family module continuation moves `PillButton`, `HubCommandButton`, `IconButton`, `HubIconButton`, and `WindowButton` out of the generic `shared.slint` bucket and into `button_components.slint` beside `HubFloatingIconButton`. `shared.slint` now stays focused on DTOs, `BrandMark`, `CenteredIcon`, navigation, status pills, and small text/panel helpers, while `button_components.slint` owns all Hub button chrome that wraps Material `FilledButton`, `TonalButton`, `FilledIconButton`, `OutlineIconButton`, `IconButton`, and `StateLayerArea`. Public imports continue through `components.slint`, and page modules import the button family from `button_components.slint` directly when they need local inheritance. `ui_inputs_contract.rs` now rejects button-family ownership drifting back into `shared.slint`; `ui_shell_composition_contract.rs`, `ui_material_usage_contract.rs`, and `ui_project_layout_contract.rs` lock the adjusted module boundaries. Verification passed with `cargo fmt --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --check`, `git diff --check`, focused direct `rustc --test` runs for those contracts, and `cargo build --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --bin zircon_hub --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never`. The real capture set is `target/hub-visual-check/button-components-extract-0601`; the Dashboard screenshot is pixel-identical to `target/hub-visual-check/nav-status-spacing-bottom18-0601` at full-frame mean/RMS `0.000/0.000`, and it still compares against `docs/ui-and-layout/hub.png` at full-frame mean/RMS `7.553/25.245`.

The non-dashboard compact-page continuation ports the verified HTML/CSS reference structure into Slint for the first non-Projects slice. `compact_page_components.slint` now owns `HubCompactTabStrip`, the shared pill-style tab row used by Settings, Cloud, Team, and catalog-style pages instead of repeating local tab chrome. Settings now opens with a reference-style General tab, a two-column Configuration Overview/Toolchain row, a Default Paths/Configuration Health row, and lower Build Defaults/Operation Timeline content; Cloud now places its package actions beside service slots under the same tab-and-metric rhythm; Team uses the same tab strip above its local identity/repository and contributor panels; Assets, Plugins, and Learn inherit the tab strip through `CatalogPage` and use compact `InfoRow` row height instead of oversized card rows. Validation for this slice passed `cargo check --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never` and `cargo build --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --bin zircon_hub --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never`. Real Windows Hub captures at `target/hub-visual-check/non-project-pages-0601/hub-settings-compact-v2.png`, `hub-cloud-compact.png`, `hub-team-compact.png`, and `hub-assets-compact-v2.png` verify the new compact tab row, panel alignment, two-column Settings/Cloud structures, and tightened catalog row density at `1568x1003`; the first Assets capture attempt failed to find a visible window, produced empty stdout/stderr logs, and passed on retry.

The catalog detail continuation ports the HTML reference's list-first plus side-detail structure into the shared Slint catalog layer instead of duplicating layout in Assets, Plugins, and Learn. `catalog_detail_components.slint` now owns `CatalogDetailPanel`, including the compact preview band, four stat cells, and three check/action rows with shared badge sizing. `CatalogPage` composes that detail panel beside `CatalogListPanel` through `WorkspacePanelSection`, keeps the existing tab strip and row delegate slot, and exposes only catalog-specific detail labels, icon, stats, and check-row state to page modules. `assets.slint`, `plugins.slint`, and `learn.slint` now supply those page-specific labels and icons while retaining the same `AssetRow`, `PluginRow`, and `LearnRow` delegates from `catalog_page_components.slint`. Validation for this slice passed `cargo check --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never` before the badge-width follow-up, then `cargo build --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --bin zircon_hub --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never` passed with output recorded in `target/hub-visual-check/catalog-detail-0601/hub-build-latest.log`; one intermediate build/check pass timed out while background `cargo`/`rustc`/`link` processes were still running and was rerun for explicit evidence. Real Windows Hub captures at `target/hub-visual-check/catalog-detail-0601/hub-assets-detail.png`, `hub-plugins-detail.png`, and `hub-learn-detail-v2.png` verify the wide `1568x1003` catalog structure, compact tab row, left list rhythm, right detail panel, and fixed long `128 resources found` badge sizing.

The catalog row continuation replaces the three catalog pages' generic `InfoRow` usage with a catalog-specific `CatalogColumnRow` in `catalog_page_components.slint`. Asset, Plugin, and Learn rows now share a fixed avatar lane, title/detail text stack, metadata column, bounded Material-style status badge, and optional circular `HubIconButton` arrow for Learn resources. This keeps long paths, plugin packaging metadata, and Learn category labels in predictable columns under the new catalog side-detail layout instead of concatenating all metadata into one `ListTile` supporting string. The same shared row preserves the compact `HubTokens.list-row-md` rhythm and reuses `HubIconButton` from `button_components.slint`, so row navigation chrome remains in the button module rather than a page-local shape. Validation passed `cargo check --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never`; `cargo build --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --bin zircon_hub --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never` passed with output recorded in `target/hub-visual-check/catalog-rows-0601/hub-build-v2.log`, after an earlier build command timed out while background compiler/linker processes continued and was rerun for explicit evidence. Real Windows Hub captures at `target/hub-visual-check/catalog-rows-0601/hub-assets-rows-v3.png`, `hub-plugins-rows-v2.png`, and `hub-learn-rows-v2.png` verify the wide catalog row alignment at `1568x1003`; an intermediate `hub-assets-rows-v2.png` capture did not navigate away from Learn and is not used as Assets evidence.

The Editor/Builds compact-tab continuation closes the next non-dashboard structure gap from the HTML reference into Slint. The web reference now renders Editor and Builds with the same compact tab strip used by Settings, Cloud, Team, and catalog pages, keeps Editor source checkout/output/profile controls on the shared `inputBox`/`comboBox` atoms, and routes Editor/Builds action stacks through the shared `actionRow` component instead of local command-card markup. The exported HTML review artifacts are `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-editor.png` and `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-builds.png`; both were regenerated after removing clipped sample rows. In Slint, `editor.slint` and `builds.slint` now consume `HubCompactTabStrip` from `compact_page_components.slint`, with Builds switching its row density to `HubTokens.list-row-sm - HubTokens.space-2` so the Source Build, action list, and Build Pipeline remain visible above the fixed status bar at `1568x1003`. Verification passed `node --check` for the changed web JS files, `node docs\ui-and-layout\hub-web-reference\validate-responsive.mjs`, `node docs\ui-and-layout\hub-web-reference\validate-interactions.mjs`, `node docs\ui-and-layout\hub-web-reference\validate-visuals.mjs`, `cargo fmt --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --check`, `cargo check --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never`, and `cargo build --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --bin zircon_hub --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never`. The Editor source-path field continuation first promotes the HTML reference's source path controls into the reusable `pathFieldRow` molecule in `material-components.js`, pairing the shared `inputBox` atom with a compact `button` action and styling the row shell in `material-styles.css`/`styles.css`. `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-editor.png` was regenerated after confirming the three Active Engine, Source Checkout, and Staged Output rows fit inside the Editor source panel without clipping. The Slint port keeps `EditorPathFieldRow` as the typed owner of the original active-engine/source/output bindings and callbacks, but changes its row shell to the same compact bordered surface and routes its action control through the shared `HubCommandButton` so Rename/Browse no longer expand into oversized teal pills. Verification passed `node --check` for `material-components.js` and `app.js`, `node docs\ui-and-layout\hub-web-reference\validate-responsive.mjs`, `node docs\ui-and-layout\hub-web-reference\validate-interactions.mjs`, `node docs\ui-and-layout\hub-web-reference\validate-visuals.mjs`, `cargo fmt --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --check`, `cargo check --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never`, `cargo test --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --test ui_inputs_contract --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never`, and `cargo build --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --bin zircon_hub --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never`. The Builds history/timeline density continuation keeps the same HTML-first workflow. `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-web-reference/app.js` now shows Builds with the four-tab Overview/Pipeline/History/Timeline strip, a right-side compact history stack capped to three rows, and a two-row Operation Timeline preview using the same shared `infoRow` component; `styles.css` adds Builds-only compact side-panel row spacing so the generated `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-builds.png` no longer clips the Timeline preview. The Slint port keeps `BuildTaskHistoryPanel` on its two `HubSection` internals but reduces its empty-history height to the passed compact row height, limits visible build-history rows to two before scrolling, and forwards `row-height: root.row-height` into each `BuildHistoryRow`. `OperationTimelinePanel` now has a compact `timeline-empty-height` and forwards the same row-height into each `OperationTimelineRow`, fixing the previous mismatch where the panel viewport budget was compact but the repeated rows still rendered at the larger default list height. The updated contracts in `ui_workspace_layout_contract.rs` and `ui_data_display_contract.rs` lock those compact empty-state and row-height bindings. Verification passed `node --check` for the web-reference JS files, `node docs\ui-and-layout\hub-web-reference\export-pages.mjs --only=hub-builds`, `node docs\ui-and-layout\hub-web-reference\validate-responsive.mjs`, `validate-interactions.mjs`, `validate-visuals.mjs`, `cargo fmt --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --check`, `cargo check --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never`, focused UI contract tests for data containers, data display, panel slots, workspace layout, and typography, and `cargo build --manifest-path zircon_hub\Cargo.toml --bin zircon_hub --locked --offline --jobs 1 --message-format short --color never`. The Learn category metadata continuation keeps the same HTML-first gate. `material-components.js` now exposes a reusable `catalogColumnRow` molecule for catalog rows that need a source column, a bounded category badge, and a trailing affordance without falling back to the generic data table. `app.js` uses that molecule for the Learn web sample, regenerating `docs/ui-and-layout/hub-learn.png` with stable source/category columns and complete category badges. The Slint port keeps the shared `CatalogColumnRow` owner in `catalog_page_components.slint`, but when `show-arrow` is true it allocates a narrower source metadata column and a wider category badge column so Learn rows no longer render a duplicated truncated `Category...` string beside the category badge. `LearnRow` now maps `metadata` directly to the resource source and `tag-text` to the resource category, while `learn.slint` reports category coverage as mixed source/folder tags in the side detail panel. `ui_selected_project_catalog_contract.rs` and `ui_page_surface_coverage_contract.rs` lock the source/category split and the current `CatalogColumnRow` ownership.
